## Pagina 3

Joe Blocchetto swallowed his bile and slowly approached.
 The motorcycle was smoking like a locomotive, and the tires were
        melted. Joe Blocchetto circled around a bit and then said:
                            –   Tires are a bit bald, aren’t they?
   –   That motorcycle has smoother tires than mine, said Pronto.
       –  What motorcycle? said Blocchetto, and turned around.
When he turned around, Pronto had already put on two new tires.”

             The Story of Pronto Soccorso and Beauty Case
                 Stefano Benni - The Bar Under the Sea, 1987

### Testo nativo breve conservato

Introduction

## Pagina 8

Letter from the President

      The Italian Motorcycling Federation publishes the twentieth edition of the Sustainability Report, a document that illustrates not only the results achieved, but also
       the vision that guides our daily commitment. 2024 marked an important milestone: 20 years of sustainability in the FMI.

     A journey that began with the 2004 Social Report and has, year after year, evolved into a mature and participatory reporting system, capable of integrating
       sporting, social, economic, and environmental dimensions. This continuity, achieved through the implementation of operational sustainability practices and
       reporting procedures, represents tangible proof of the seriousness with which the Federation has chosen to face the challenges of the present and the future,
       with courage and transparency.

      To confirm this commitment, in 2024 Sport e Salute recognized the FMI as the first Sports Federation for ESG Rating. A result that is not only a source of pride,
       but also a responsibility: to be a benchmark in Italian sport and to demonstrate that sustainability and sport are deeply interconnected.

      2024 was also a significant year for the sustainability of federal activities, particularly regarding the organization of events such as the Italian Circuit Racing
      Championship (CIV) and the FIM Rally.
      The CIV obtained ISO 20121 certification for its event management system, an acknowledgment that confirms FMI’s ability to organize an entire sports
      championship according to the highest international sustainability standards.6
       At the same time, the FIM Rally, an international tourism event, was organized and managed according to social, environmental, and economic sustainability
         criteria, showing how sport can be a vehicle of universal values and genuine attention to the territory and local communities.

         All these achievements have taught us that the sustainability of motorcycling sport can only be realized where entire communities are involved in projects
      capable of leaving a legacy both for the Federation itself and for the regions. This result would not have been possible without the contribution of our Moto Clubs,
       volunteers, institutional and commercial partners, and every single motorcyclist who shares the Federation’s values.

      Looking to the future, we know that the real challenge is to continue growing as a sports community, guided by responsibility towards future generations as our
      compass. With this awareness, we commit every day to working for an inclusive, safe, and sustainable motorcycling sport.


                                                                                                                                        Giovanni Copioli Esq.
                                                                                                                     President of the Italian Motorcycling Federation





       FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 9

SECTION 0 | Introduction





              7

## Pagina 10

Methodology
                                   The Sustainability Report confirms its role as a fundamental tool for guiding the development of an effective
                                     governance model, capable of rebuilding the value chain, coordinating stakeholder relationships, and
                                               carefully planning reporting processes. At FMI, social responsibility related to sustainable development is
                                        not seen as a mere bureaucratic requirement, but rather as an opportunity to rethink welfare and imagine a
                                new future for the motorcycle racing community.


                                        For 20 years, FMI has consistently measured its performance, initially with the Social Report, focusing on the      social and
                                    economic impact generated, and, since 2017, with the Sustainability Report, which also emphasizes environmental issues.

## Pagina 12

Drafting principles               The following drafting principles, set forth in the guidelines referred to in Article 14, paragraph 1, of the Third Sector Code,
                                      adopted by decree of 4 July 2019 of the Ministry of Labour and Social Policies, have, in their progressive interpretation,
                                          represented an undoubtedly valuable compass for the correct drafting of the Sustainability Report.

                                                                                                     All useful information that could potentially influence stakeholders’ assessments has                                              Relevance
                                                                  been included without omissions, justifying any limitations or exclusions.

                                                                                                     All the main stakeholders were identified (limiting the user base) and therefore only
                                           Completeness              the information necessary to provide guidance for their evaluation of organizational
                                                                       performance was included.
                                              Transparency            The criteria used to collect and classify information have been clarified.

                                                                  The information was presented in an unbiased manner, thus documenting both positive                                                        Neutrality
                                                                  and negative aspects.
10                                              Reporting period               Activities and results for the reference year 2024 have been documented.

                                                                      Data that allows for temporal (changes over time) and spatial (comparing the data
                                                Comparability              with that of other similar sports organizations) comparisons have been included where
                                                                                   possible.

                                                         A language accessible even to readers without specific technical expertise has been                                                            Clarity
                                                                           used.
                                       Accuracy and verifiability        Reference has been made, where possible, to the information sources used.

                                                                            Overestimations or underestimations were avoided, and uncertain data were not                                                             Reliability
                                                                          presented as if they were certain.

                                                                  The third parties involved guaranteed autonomy and independence in the transparent                                    Autonomy of third parties                                                                               preparation of the Sustainability Report.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 13

SECTION 0 | Introduction



Utilised Guidelines              The processes and milestones that led to the historic adoption of the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development and its
                              17 Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) in 2015 represented the starting point for recognizing sport’s contribution to
                                         sustainability.

                                      37. “Sport is also an important enabler of sustainable development. We recognize the growing contribution of sport
                                       to the realization of development and peace in its promotion of tolerance and respect and the contributions it makes
                                       to the empowerment of women and of young people, individuals, and communities as well as to health, education,
                               and social inclusion objectives”

                                   (2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development, A / RES / 70/1, paragraph 37).

                              The robustness of FMI’s Sustainability Report methodological framework, capable of integrating multiple international
                                frameworks and standards, was reaffirmed in 2024.

                                         In addition to the principles set forth in Ministerial Decree of 4 July 2019, the framework developed over time in line with the
                                   Guidelines formulated by the Laboratory of Economics and Management of the University of Rome “Foro Italico” was also
                                   confirmed, with reference to the G.B.S. (2013) standard, drafted by the National Association for Scientific Research on Social    11
                                    Reporting, for the accurate determination of added value.

                              The UNI ISO 26000 Guideline, in its latest version of October 2020, continues to serve as a compass for developing the social
                                        responsibility dimension in a voluntary and structured manner.

                                                          It is also worth noting the partial adoption of the AccountAbility 1000 (AA1000 – 2018 edition) process standard, particularly
                              used for stakeholder mapping and their engagement in defining material issues, as well as in the subsequent identification of
                                       specific Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) designed to assess and measure the Entity’s activities and objectives.

                             As already mentioned, for several years now the 17 United Nations Sustainable Development Goals have become an integral
                                     part of FMI’s ethical approach. These 17 goals have been progressively embedded to reinforce an integrated vision of sports
                               development across its economic, social, and environmental dimensions. The implementation of the 2030 Agenda has
                                     consistently inspired stakeholder engagement, promoting a development model for FMI capable of driving a sustainability
                                     strategy that advances a selected number of SDGs on multiple fronts—through sports, social, and environmental projects—
                                   ensuring a broader impact that goes beyond the economic dimension.

## Pagina 14

United nations’ sustainable       The SDGs closest to the FMI targets have been confirmed and highlighted in the following figure with an applicability mark (v).
       development goals and the
      FMI





12





                                         Sport is a universal language that unites peoples, cultures, and genders to transform our society. With this in mind, in 2017, during
                                           the Sixth International Conference of Ministers and Senior Officials Responsible for Physical Education and Sport (MINEPS VI),
                             UNESCO implemented the Kazan Plan, a global agreement linking sports policies and the Sustainable Development Goals.

                                                  Specifically, the FMI addresses the following SDGs in several ways, which have been summarized below.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 15

SECTION 0 | Introduction



Goal 3. Ensure healthy lives and promote well-being for all at all ages.
To promote an active lifestyle that enhances well-being and health, prevents disease, and provides social, psychological, and
physiological benefits; to encourage the demand for the sustainable consumption of goods and services.

Goal 4. Ensure inclusive and equitable quality education and promote lifelong learning opportunities for all
To disseminate the knowledge and skills necessary to promote sustainable development, including through education aimed
at fostering sustainable development and lifestyles, human rights, gender equality, and the promotion of a culture of social
responsibility for sustainable development.

Goal 5. Achieve gender equality and empower all women and girls.
To ensure gender equality, the effective participation of women, and equal leadership opportunities at every decision-making
level and in all areas of Federation life.
Goal 8. Promote sustained, inclusive, and sustainable economic growth, full and productive employment, and decent work
for all.
To promote inclusive and sustainable economic growth by creating opportunities for all and developing professional integration                                                                                   13skills; to enhance the effective and responsible management of volunteers, contributing to encouraging their participation in
society and commitment to the community.
Goal 10. Reduce inequality within and among countries
To uphold equality, diversity, and inclusion for all, regardless of age, sex, race, ethnicity, origin, sexual orientation, gender
identity, religion, economic status, or any other factor; to address inequality by empowering individuals and communities.

Goal 12. Ensure sustainable consumption and production patterns.
To support sustainable production and consumption practices and encourage lifestyles that respect nature, including through
education and awareness campaigns regarding sporting goods, events, and related services.

Goal 13. Take urgent action to combat climate change and its impacts by regulating emissions and promoting developments
in renewable energy
To contribute to combating climate change through sustainable projects and education and awareness campaigns, encouraging
the adoption of concrete behaviors to promote the responsible use of natural resources.

## Pagina 16

Goal 15. Protect, restore and promote sustainable use of terrestrial ecosystems, sustainably manage forests, combat
                                               desertification, and halt and reverse land degradation and halt biodiversity loss
                                       To promote the protection and sustainable use of terrestrial ecosystems, including through activities and messages aimed at
                                        encouraging environmentally responsible practices; to highlight best practices for protecting ecosystems in the construction
                                    and management of sports facilities and events.
                                      Goal 17. Strengthen the means of implementation and revitalize the global partnership for sustainable development.
                                       To create synergies, networks, and multi-stakeholder partnerships to support the achievement of sustainable development
                                             goals; to collaboratively measure the progress of sustainable development by evaluating and reporting on sporting, economic,
                                                 social, and environmental impacts.

      The new European regulation       Directive 2464/2022 – Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD), the new European regulation on sustainability
      on sustainability reporting –         reporting by EU companies, entered into force on January 5, 2023. The CSRD aims to promote transparency in the European
     CSRD                                single market and harmonize sustainability reporting processes through common standards and clear performance indicators.
                                    The FMI has begun studying the new regulation, with particular reference to the Voluntary Standard for Non-Listed Small
                                    and Medium-Sized Undertakings (VSME) – the voluntary reporting standard for unlisted small and medium-sized enterprises
                                      updated by EFRAG at the end of 2024 – seeking useful elements to improve its reporting system and new avenues for dialogue
14                                         with its partners. However, since the debate on the application of the regulation is still ongoing, it was decided not to draw on
                                                                           it for this edition of the document, but to continue the study and in-depth analysis with a view to future reporting.


       Reporting scope and            The 2024 Sustainability Report considered all activities with a significant operational impact (material) and stakeholder interest.
        reporting process                To progressively strengthen a voluntary, mature, and informed approach to social responsibility, the involvement of senior
                                   management was confirmed, emphasizing the crucial role of governance, which serves both as a guiding and policy tool and
                                       as a fundamental aspect of social responsibility, as highlighted in UNI ISO 26000.
                                    The materiality analysis is the result of a process coordinated by a dedicated working group and which involved, first, federal
                                       governance and then internal stakeholders (with particular reference to personnel) and external stakeholders (especially
                                       sponsors and partners). The topics deemed relevant and significant were evaluated and confirmed for 2024.

                                    The Federation is working on an update of the topics in 2025, which will involve renewed stakeholder engagement and will see
                                           the FMI interface with dual materiality.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 24

The Italian Motorcycling        The FMI is one of the 50 National Sports Federations recognized by CONI and is “the only body authorized to discipline,
       Federation                          regulate, and manage the sport of motorcycling in Italy and to represent  it internationally. It has technical, organizational,
                                    and management autonomy, under the supervision of Italian National Olympic Committee”. It brings together in a single,
                                            non-partisan, apolitical, and non-profit association all the regularly affiliated clubs, sports associations, and other similar
                                              recreational-sporting organizations that practice motorcycling in  Italy.  It  is the sole representative of the International
                                          Motorcycling Federation (FIM) and FIM Europe.

       Mission and Vision            MISSION
                                    The FMI develops, monitors, promotes, and protects all motorcycling activities, both sporting and otherwise, throughout
                                                       Italy by organizing initiatives aimed at promoting a culture of road safety and education, protecting the nation’s motorcycling
                                               heritage, and respecting the rules, people, and the environment, in line with the principles of transparency and economic
                                                  efficiency.

                                     VISION
                                    FMI draws inspiration from the needs of both competitive and recreational motorcyclists to create satisfaction, new opportunities,
                                    and safety standards, aiming to become the benchmark for Italian motorcycling.
22

## Pagina 26

Values                           Values are the ideals in which the Federation believes; they are the foundation of our organization’s life and actions. They
                                           represent beliefs, convictions about what is desirable, and they provide criteria for evaluating the behaviour of all stakeholders.
                                          Values inspire the FMI and its management, and for this very reason, it is important to codify and write them down so that
                                                 internal stakeholders can embrace and internalize them, and external stakeholders can use them as criteria for judging the
                                      company’s awareness and commitment to social responsibility, verifying their adherence and consistency over time.

     Code of Ethics                The FMI Code of Ethics, approved by the Federal management  Council on 26 July 2019, is a regulation that sets out the
                                           general principles and guidelines required for the exercise of every federal role and necessary for the proper and correct
                                       conduct of activities, as well as to ensure the reliability of the Federation’s management and public image.
                                    The Code, which complements the existing CONI Code of Sports Conduct, aims to guide the behaviour of all members of
                                           the Federation according to principles of compliance with laws and federal regulations, loyalty, professional integrity, and
                                     economic efficiency, both in internal relations and in dealings with external stakeholders.

     A sustainable governance:       Legislative Decree No. 231 of 8 June 2001, , which came into force on 4 July of the same year, marked a significant innovation
       the organisational,                  in the Italian legal system, introducing administrative liability for legal entities, companies, and associations, including those
      management and control        without legal personality. This measure responded to the need to align the national legal framework with Italy’s international
      model (MOG 231)              commitments in the fight against corruption and organised crime, in line with the recommendations contained in various
                                                international conventions, including the OECD Convention on Combating Bribery of Foreign Public Officials, the UN Convention
                                            against Transnational Organized Crime and the directives of the European Union.
                                                  In addition, the adoption of Legislative Decree No. 24/2023 on ‘whistleblowing’ represents a further development in this
                                             regulatory framework. The decree establishes the obligation for organisations to set up internal reporting channels that ensure
                                           the confidentiality of the whistleblower and protect them from retaliation. Safeguards are provided against retaliatory measures
                                      such as dismissal, denial of promotion or discrimination, thereby protecting those who report violations. Furthermore, an external
                                             reporting channel managed by the National Anti-Corruption Authority (ANAC) has been established, to which whistleblowers
                                may turn if impartiality or security of the internal reporting process cannot be guaranteed.
      WHISTLEBLOWING           On 23 June 2023, the FMI Federal management Council established the Organism of Supervision (OdV), tasked with




        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 29

SECTION 1 | Identity and Governance



ensuring compliance with and updating of the Organisational, Management and Control Model (MOG) pursuant to Legislative Decree 231/2001.

In the course of 2023, the OdV also presented to the FMI Federal management Council a multi-year plan providing for targeted control, information and communication
activities, aimed at strengthening overall compliance with the MOG.
Since its establishment and up to 31 December 2024, the OdV met 14 times, 8 of which in 2024:
•   September 27th, 2023
•   October 9th, 2023
•   October 24th, 2023
•  November 8th, 2023
•  November 15th, 2023
•   December 22nd, 2023
•   January 15th, 2024
•   January 24th, 2024
•   February 16th, 2024
•   March 1st, 2024
•    April 5th, 2024                                                                                                          27•  May 21st, 2024
•   July 9th, 2024
•   September 25th, 2024

During these meetings, the members of the OdV considered it appropriate to review the Organisational, Management and Control Model, introducing the necessary
amendments to the protocols contained in the document, also considering the ongoing legislative developments.
In addition to updating the protocols included in the special section of the MOG, the OdV also defined the information flows that offices are required to send to the
OdV throughout the year, with frequencies determined according to the type of activity concerned.

To this end, a dedicated e-mail address was created for all communications relating to the Federation’s MOG activities. The OdV also adopted its own Rules of
Procedure, aimed at defining the internal functioning of the Body and the respective responsibilities of its members.
In recent months, the OdV has carried out a careful assessment in view of the training activities to be provided to employees, managers, and members of the FMI
Management Council, as required by Legislative Decree 231/2001 and as incorporated in the Federation’s MOG.
In particular, specific training sessions were delivered to employees and coordinators – with the participation of OdV members and the Deputy Secretary General –
and separately to members of the FMI Management Council.
At the conclusion of the course, all participants were given the opportunity to review the training materials and recordings of the sessions.
Finally, on 25 October 2024, the Federal Council approved the updated version of the MOG, which was subsequently shared with the relevant stakeholders.

## Pagina 32

Institutional activities -           The First 2024 Meeting of the FIM Board of Directors in Lyon
       Year 2024
                                    The FIM Board of Directors met on Wednesday 14 and Thursday 15 February 2024 at the Palais des Congrès in Lyon, France,
                                                   just a few days before the weekend’s FIM Commissions Conference. In attendance, as FIM Vice President, was the FMI
                                             President, Giovanni Copioli.

                                         During the meeting, the leadership of the Fédération Internationale de Motocyclisme (FIM) discussed all disciplines. Particular
                                              attention was given to the promotion of the Flat Track, Sidecar and Sidecarcross World Championships, including the
                                          Sidecarcross and Quadcross of Nations. The Board also addressed amendments to the TrialGP regulations and established a
                                         dedicated Working Group on Trial.

                                    The Council reaffirmed its commitment to authorising the use of electric motorcycles in Supercross, with specific regulations
                                              to be drafted by the Sports Commissions. Another key focus was the Intercontinental Games, a new sporting competition
                                              featuring teams divided by continent, scheduled to take place in Jerez de la Frontera, Spain, from 29 November to 1 December
                                        2024.

                                    The Board also reiterated its closeness and solidarity with all those suffering as a result of the Russian invasion of Ukraine,30                                           confirming the decisions taken during the extraordinary session of 5 March 2022.

                                    The FIM presented the 2023 financial statements and the 2024 budget forecast, both reflecting the Federation’s solid financial
                                                position. Preliminary statistical data were also presented on motorcycle-related injuries, collected through a dedicated
                                          monitoring platform developed for this purpose.

                                                     Finally, the Board approved – and subsequently signed during the Commissions Conference – the agreement with Amicale
                                                    Spirit of Speed for the FIM certification of Historic Track Motorcycles.

                                  An update was shared on ongoing initiatives and networking activities (such as webinars and shared guidelines) between
                                            National Federations and Continental Unions. The logo and promotional video for the FIM’s 120th anniversary celebrations
                                      were unveiled. The Board approved amendments to the Code of Motorcycle Gatherings. Furthermore, the Commission
                                             Directors were given the opportunity to present their most significant projects. The revised FIM Gender Equality Guidelines,
                                         presented by the Women’s Commission, were approved. Over the weekend, all FIM Commissions held their meetings. Two
                                           plenary sessions were organised: one dedicated to Superlicence seminars, and the other to the initiatives celebrating the FIM’s
                                        120th anniversary in 2024. A further session addressed the question: “Why are diversity and inclusion important in sport?”
                                Among the speakers were FIM Vice President and FMI President, Giovanni Copioli, in his capacity as Chair of the Awards
                                      Committee, and Simone Folgori, Head of the Italian Circuit Racing Championship (CIV), during the above-mentioned round
                                               table.



        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 34

FIM Commissions Conference in Lyon – Three Days of Meetings and Discussions

       The FIM Commissions Conference took place in Lyon from 16 to 18 February. The event opened with a plenary session on the Superlicence introduced by FIM
        President Jorge Viegas. Topics addressed included: the campaign to reduce noise emissions, the second phase of the helmet homologation programme, the
       development of airbags, and the detection and management of concussions.

      On Saturday 17 February, the Plenary Session of Commission Members opened with the presentation of initiatives and projects planned for the FIM’s 120th
        anniversary. These included the inauguration of the new headquarters and Motorcycle Sport Museum, the FIM Hospitality Unit, a commemorative book, the new
      FIM brand identity, a virtual mosaic portraying much of the “FIM family”, and the organisation of the Intercontinental Games.

      FIM CEO Françoise Emery delivered a keynote speech entitled “Why are diversity and inclusion in sport important?” and later joined a round table discussion on
        the subject with Sara Mariani (FIA Director for Sustainability, Diversity and Inclusion), Simone Folgori (Coordinator of the FMI Sports Department, Member of the
      FIM Circuit Racing Commission and Permanent Steward for Superbike) and Janika Judeika (Director of the FIM Women’s Commission). On this occasion, Folgori
         illustrated initiatives aimed at developing the Women’s CIV (Women’s Italian Circuit Racing Championship reserved for female riders) and presented the project on
        motorcycling beyond the gender gap.

       During the three-day conference, the International Motorcycling Federation also presented awards to the FIM Honorary Members appointed during the General32       Assembly.

       The programme on Sunday 18 February focused on the first FIM Medical Summit, which brought together doctors and representatives from all sporting disciplines.
       Updates were provided on medical and anti-doping regulations across disciplines, along with the presentation of the FIM insurance programme for riders and the
       second phase of the helmet homologation programme. The development and use of airbags were also discussed.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 35

SECTION 1 | Identity and Governance



CONI – Italian Olympic Committee Awards Fabrizio Meoni the Gold Medal for Athletic Valour

An emotional day for motorcycling, which took centre stage before the highest representatives of Italian sport. During the National Council meeting held on 17 April
2024 at the “Giulio Onesti” Olympic Training Centre in Rome, CONI awarded Fabrizio Meoni the Gold Medal for Athletic Valour – a prestigious honour bestowed
upon the generous rider, who tragically passed away during the 2005 Dakar Rally, in recognition of his sporting achievements and life values.
The Minister for Sport, Andrea Abodi, CONI President Giovanni Malagò, and FMI President and Council Member Giovanni Copioli presented the medal and a
commemorative certificate to Fabrizio’s wife, Elena, and his son, Gioele. Both movingly recalled the vital role of solidarity in the life of Meoni, winner of the 2001
and 2002 Dakar Rallies. Also in attendance were CONI Vice Presidents Silvia Salis and Claudia Giordani, CONI Secretary General Carlo Mornati, and the Mayor of
Castiglion Fiorentino, Mario Agnelli.

The award, which honoured the memory of Fabrizio Meoni, was promoted by the FMI and supported by Minister Abodi, who in January visited Castiglion Fiorentino
(AR) and had the opportunity to gain experience more about the legacy of the much-loved rider. A commemorative video retracing Fabrizio’s extraordinary
achievements introduced the most heartfelt moments of the ceremony.

In his speech, Minister Abodi highlighted how awarding the Gold Medal for Athletic Valour celebrates the values of family and altruism, which fully embodied Meoni’s
spirit. CONI President Malagò also emphasised that Meoni’s moral and sporting stature were perfectly united in his legacy. Fabrizio’s son Gioele and wife Elena
recalled his profound sense of solidarity and love for Africa, which found expression in numerous charitable initiatives.                               33

## Pagina 36

The Second 2024 FIM Board of Directors Meeting in Mies

      On Friday 3 and Saturday 4 May, the FIM Board of Directors convened in Mies,
      home of the Fédération Internationale de Motocyclisme. FMI President Giovanni
        Copioli attended in his capacity as FIM Vice President.

      A wide range of topics were addressed. From a sporting perspective, particular
       focus was given to Liberty Media’s acquisition of Dorna Sports. The FIM
        highlighted the success of the Women’s Circuit Racing World Championship,
       which recorded 40 applications, with 24 riders selected to line up on the grid.
       The Board expressed its support for the creation of the Classic Motorcycle
      Commission (CMC), aimed at promoting vintage motorcycle sport. Details of the
         Intercontinental Games in Jerez were also presented.

         In line with the highest standards of clean sport, the FIM announced its cooperation
        with the International Testing Agency (ITA), gaining access to additional resources,
        expert advice, and technical assistance in the field of anti-doping.34
       The Board also emphasised the crucial role of Continental Unions  in the
       development of both sporting and non-sporting activities. An MoU was agreed with
        the International Union for Conservation of Nature (IUCN). Updates were shared on
        the progress of the FIM Sport Museum. The Board approved amendments to the
        Statutes, Regulations and Sporting Code in view of the 2024 General Assembly,
       as well as changes to the Environmental Code. The 2023 Financial Statements
       were approved for presentation at the General Assembly in Palma de Mallorca
        (Spain) on 6 December 2024, alongside the 2024 budget forecast.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 37

SECTION 1 | Identity and Governance



Meeting with the Italian Minister of Tourism, Daniela Santanchè and the FMI President Giovanni Copioli.

On Thursday 9 May, at the Ministry of Tourism in Rome, an important institutional meeting was held between Minister Daniela Santanchè and the President of
the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI), Giovanni Copioli. The discussions focused on motorcycle tourism and the opportunities it offers. Issues of interest for
motorcyclists and motorcycle tourists in general were also shared.
“Motorcycle tourism”, declared Minister of Tourism Daniela Santanchè, “represents an important resource for the entire tourism sector, offering unique and
engaging experiences while contributing to the diversification of the tourism offer across seasons. This form of tourism has the potential to enhance lesser-known
areas, supporting the economic and sustainable development of rural and peripheral regions. It is essential to underline the need to foster a culture of road safety,
with educational initiatives across different age groups, including Advanced Safe Riding Courses dedicated to adult users as required by law. Developing dedicated
itineraries for motorcyclists and implementing specialised services not only enriches a Region’s tourism offer but also strengthens its attractiveness in international
markets.”

“I thank the Minister of Tourism, Daniela Santanchè, for her openness and sensitivity to our issues and for the consideration she shows towards motorcyclists,” stated
FMI President Giovanni Copioli. “During the meeting we highlighted some of the key strengths of motorcycle tourism: beyond the economic benefits it generates for
local communities, it allows for the discovery of places unknown to mass tourism. This shared vision is essential to continue promoting the organisation of national
and international events by the FMI, which contribute to the enhancement of Italian locations and itineraries, even lesser-known ones, throughout the year. I conclude
by expressing my satisfaction with the Minister’s attention to road safety, an issue on which the FMI places great importance.”                           35



Federal Training Center of Faenza. Operational agreement signed between FMI and Moto Club 04 Park

On Thursday, 30 May, the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) and Moto Club 04 Park signed the operational agreement governing the concession of premises
designated as an FMI Training Center within the Monte Coralli facility. Present at the historic track were the Deputy Mayor of Faenza, Andrea Fabbri, FMI President
Giovanni Copioli, and Andrea Dovizioso representing the club.

This agreement details the general framework of the convention signed in 2023 between the FMI and the Municipality of Faenza. The Federal Training Center will
consist of an office, a large classroom, and a motorcycle storage facility. It will be primarily dedicated to off-road activities and will serve as a reference point both
for the collegiate gatherings of the Pata Talenti Azzurri and for the many educational projects promoted by the FMI. Furthermore, the Federation will be able to use
the track exclusively on several dates to carry out field initiatives, particularly those aimed at youth development and talent scouting.

## Pagina 38

Minister of Infrastructure and Transport Matteo Salvini meets FMI President Giovanni Copioli

      On Tuesday, 21 May, a meeting took place in Rome at the Ministry of Infrastructure and Transport between Minister Matteo Salvini and Giovanni Copioli, President
        of the Italian Motorcycling Federation. In a spirit of great openness, President Copioli was able to present to the Minister the significance of Italian motorcycling in
       terms of its user base and the key role of powered two-wheelers in easing urban traffic congestion.
       The meeting also addressed the government’s reform of the Highway Code, already approved by the Chamber of Deputies and currently under consideration by the
        Senate. Among the provisions included in the reform are: the recognition of motorcyclists as vulnerable road users, the installation of motorcycle safety devices
      on guardrails, and the possibility for motorcycles over 120 cc (and 6 kW if electric) to access motorways. These issues are of major interest to motorcyclists, and
        the FIM expressed its hope that the reform will soon complete the legislative process and become law.
       Another key topic was the status of historic and collectible vehicles, which embody the heritage and prestige of Italy, given that many motorcycle brands were
       born in the country and deserve to be protected. President Copioli also raised concerns about recently introduced insurance obligations applying even to non-
         circulating and historic vehicles, which seem to go beyond what the European Insurance Directive intended for Member States. Finally, he expressed regret that
        recent upgrades to the Monza Circuit did not include the measures necessary to make it suitable for hosting motorcycle competitions.
      FMI President Giovanni Copioli stated: “I thank the Minister for his hospitality and for having listened attentively to the issues we presented. Italian motorcycling36          is an excellence worldwide from a cultural, sporting, and economic standpoint, and therefore deserves proper consideration. I hope that the recognition of our
         vulnerability and the incentive for road owners to install safety guardrails will soon become a consolidated reality. I also asked the Minister to maintain strong attention
       on motorcycling users. My gratitude also extends to Senator Stefano Corti and MP Giovanni Tombolato, who contributed to making this meeting possible.”



       Piedmont Region and FMI Together for Major Sporting Events

       The Italian Grand Prix, held in Maggiora (NO) on 15–16 June 2024, marked the halfway point of the Motocross World Championship and represented an opportunity
        to highlight the strong relationship between the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) and the Piedmont Region. This was underlined by the President of the Region,
        Alberto Cirio, in a video message addressed to the Maggiora Park staff, to the family of Emilio Ostorero (the unforgettable Piedmontese champion), and to the FMI.
      FMI President Giovanni Copioli also emphasized the close bond between the Federation and the Piedmont Region: “I thank President Alberto Cirio for his kind
       words towards us, towards Maggiora Park and the Ostorero family. His statements bear witness to his attention to motorcycling which, as he himself underlined,
        brings major events and many related benefits, both economic and otherwise, to the territory. Collaboration between institutions is fundamental from every point of
        view, and finding such a close relationship with the Piedmont Region is for us a source of pride and motivation. We will continue to work so that our sport may be a
       source of opportunity for the country.”

SECTION 1 | Identity and Governance



“Ride to Close the Gender Gap,” the prestigious panel held in Misano

“Ride to Close the Gender Gap” èwas the theme of the panel held on Friday, 14 June, at the Misano World Circuit Marco Simoncelli, during the Italian round of the
FIM Superbike World Championship – Pirelli Emilia-Romagna Round. On this occasion, the FIM Women’s Circuit Racing World Championship (WorldWCR) was
officially inaugurated, the first world championship in track racing reserved for female riders.
The FIM has launched a series of actions to develop strategies aimed at promoting women’s participation in two-wheeled sports.
A distinguished group of speakers contributed to the debate: FMI President Jorge Viegas; Giovanni Copioli, FMI Vice President and FIM President; Damiano
Zamana, FMI Deputy General Management; Janika Judeika, FMI Women’s Commission Director; David Bosser, WorldSBK Sporting Business Development
Senior Manager; Paolo Pavesio, Marketing Director Yamaha Motor Europe N.V. Motorsport; Nancy Van de Ven, Women’s Motocross World Champion (WMX);
and Katja Poensgen, former Grand Prix rider. Guest of honour was Italian Senator Domenica Spinelli.
The FIM Women’s Circuit Racing World Championship consists of six rounds, with 24 participants representing 19 different nationalities, all competing on Yamaha
YZF-R7 machines.

## Pagina 40

“FIM Europe Policy Conference on Motorcycle Activities”

      On Friday, 5 July, the FIM Europe Policy Conference on Motorcycle Activities was held in Fiumicino (Rome), at the Hilton Hotel. The conference highlighted
        best practices to bring every national motorcycling federation closer to their national and European political representatives. The meeting had a dual purpose: to
       encourage this kind of activity across all European federations and to highlight the strength of an organization such as FIM Europe, which includes 49 member
        federations.
       The event opened with welcome remarks from FIM President Jorge Viegas and FIM Europe President Michał Sikora. This was followed by two panel discussions:
        the first focused on FIM Europe’s policies and institutional relations within the continent, as well as on the upcoming challenges to be faced by the newly elected
       European Parliament.
       The second panel concentrated on best practices from the Italian, French, and Dutch motorcycling federations. FMI President Giovanni Copioli highlighted the
        important and ongoing activities of the Italian Federation. In his detailed address, Copioli stressed the importance of collaboration with institutions at both national
       and local levels to ensure the continuity of motorcycling activities, both sporting and non-sporting.
       The FMI’s institutional cooperation efforts were further reinforced by the presence of Senator Elena Murelli—who briefly presented the activities of parliamentary
       groups established in support of motorcycles—and by Federico Vitale, Head of the Technical Area and International Relations at ANCMA.
        President Copioli also illustrated the many institutions with which the FMI has cooperation agreements and joint projects, including: the Ministry of Infrastructure
       and Transport, the Ministry of Tourism, the Ministry of Education, the Carabinieri Corps, ANCI Motori, ISPRA, Civil Protection, Firefighters, and Forestry Carabinieri.
      He also summarized the draft legislation to amend the Highway Code, on which the FMI is working intensively, aimed at recognizing motorcyclists as vulnerable38       road users.
       The conference concluded with a focus on active and passive safety, introduced by a demonstration of how an airbag works.

## Pagina 42

Press Conference on the Presentation of the Bill on Motor Tourism

       ‘Enhancing Italian motor brands and locations, known and appreciated worldwide, to strengthen our country’s tourism offering’. This is the goal of the bipartisan
            bill ‘Provisions to Promote the Development of Motor Tourism’. The initiative was presented on Wednesday, 17 July, at the Chamber of Deputies by the first
         signatories, Hon. Stefano Vaccari (PD) and Senator Gianni Berrino (FdI), respectively President and Vice President of the Parliamentary Intergroup ‘Friends of
        Motors’, accompanied by Deputies Mauro D’Attis (FI, Secretary) and Andrea Dara (Lega), as well as Senator Costanzo Della Porta (FdI) from the Intergroup’s
       Bureau. The proposal was developed in collaboration with Città dei Motori, represented at the conference by President Luigi Zironi and Secretary General Danilo
        Moriero.
       Speakers included the President of the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI), Giovanni Copioli, ASI (Automotoclub Storico Italiano) board members Costanzo
         Truini and Riccardo Zavatti, and, via video message, the President of ACI (Automobile Club d’Italia), Angelo Sticchi Damiani. Senator Erika Stefani, Coordinator
        of the Parliamentary Intergroup for Motorcycle Mobility, was also present.
       During the press conference, FMI President Giovanni Copioli stated: “I believe this bipartisan bill can be an excellent tool to promote Motor Tourism, a sector on
       which the Italian Motorcycling Federation places great importance. It is an activity that allows the discovery of all types of territories, including those unknown to
      mass tourism. Moreover, it brings significant economic benefits to the areas it passes through. I hope these initiatives will further highlight motorcycle tourism, even
      more than they already do. It is immersive, slow, and has little environmental impact.”
      Key highlights of the bill include: the establishment of a national consultative body and an annual fund for motor tourism; the international promotion of the Italian
       Motor Week, the week dedicated to Italian motor heritage; the introduction of the “Checkered Flag” certification for municipalities capable of attracting national and40         international tourist flows by valorising Italian brands; and the approval of a national calendar of motor events by the Ministry of Tourism.

SECTION 1 | Identity and Governance



The new FIM headquarters inaugurated

On Thursday, October 10th, the Fédération Internationale de Motocyclisme inaugurated its new headquarters in Mies, Switzerland. President Jorge Viegas cut the
ribbon in the presence of 400 guests. In attendance were FMI President and FIM Vice President Giovanni Copioli, the promoters of the FIM Championships and
World Cups, legendary champions—including fifteen-time world champion Giacomo Agostini—important local and international media, partners, and FIM members.
Before the official inauguration, guests enjoyed a performance by two great trials riders outside the new headquarters: Toni Bou, 36-time world champion, and
Emma Bristow, 10-time women’s world champion. The design of the new FIM headquarters embodies the essence of motorcycling, reflecting the values and spirit
of the sport, along with the brotherhood, collaboration, and communication that unite the passionate motorcycling community. FIM has prioritized environmentally
friendly building materials by selecting low-carbon and bio-based options.

## Pagina 46

FIM and FMI Received in Audience by Pope Francis to Celebrate the 120th Anniversary of the Fédération Internationale de Motocyclisme

      On the occasion of the 120th anniversary of the Fédération Internationale de Motocyclisme (FIM), on Monday, 25 November, Pope Francis received FIM and FMI in
       a Private Audience at the Vatican.
         In the Sala dei Papi of the Apostolic Palace, the Holy Father welcomed CONI President Giovanni Malagò, FIM President Jorge Viegas, FMI President Giovanni
         Copioli, three-time World Champion Francesco Bagnaia, and other representatives of both Federations.
         In his address, the Pontiff particularly emphasized the social role of the two Federations: “The promotion of road education – also foreseen in your Statutes through
        the organization of courses in schools – deserves recognition. In this way, you have the opportunity to make young people reflect: they admire motorcycle champions
        but are often unaware of the dangers,” the Holy Father underlined, before continuing: “This is why the time dedicated to educational initiatives in this regard is an
        investment in life itself. Thank you for what you do to help and educate in this direction!”
         In conclusion, Pope Francis added with a smile: “And please, pray for me as well, because I need prayers. Why? Because my work is very accelerated, and my
       motorbike is old and not working well.”
       Here below the specific statements:
       Giovanni Malagò, CONI President: “It was an honor to participate in the Pope’s audience together with the Italian and International Motorcycling Federations, led
       by Presidents Giovanni Copioli and Jorge Viegas, with the presence of our champion Pecco Bagnaia, to reaffirm the constant commitment of these two institutional
         entities in promoting this fantastic discipline, starting from safety and the values that are indispensable elements. A mission fully shared, inspiring the strategic
        guidelines of CONI and the entire movement, with the aim of transmitting to young people – the same ones mentioned by the Holy Father – the most important44        sporting principles, making them the foundation of healthy and harmonious growth.”
       Jorge Viegas, FIM President: “In this very special year in which the FIM celebrates its 120th anniversary, we had an emotional meeting with Pope Francis, who
      welcomed us in the Vatican with humanity and love. Together we prayed for the entire motorcycling community and especially for the FIM family, whether Catholic
        or not. I thank the Italian Motorcycling Federation and its President, Giovanni Copioli, for this incredible opportunity.”
       Francesco Bagnaia, three-time World Champion: “It was a great honor to be received by the Holy Father, a unique occasion that I will always carry with me. I was
       deeply struck by his charisma and his words, with which he highlighted his attention to young people and the very young. Together with the Italian Motorcycling
        Federation and the International Motorcycling Federation, we shared a moment that marked me both as a man and as an athlete.”

## Pagina 48

Restyling of the FMI Logo

      A historic event that testifies to the ongoing renewal of the Italian Motorcycling Federation. The restyling of the Italian Motorcycling Federation’s logo was presented in
        Milan on Tuesday, October 29, 2024, at the Alberto Bonacossa Tennis Club. The conference, held at the club named after the president of the Moto Club d’Italia (later
        the FMI) from 1914 to 1931, was attended by numerous representatives of institutions, industry, and the press. FMI President Giovanni Copioli opened the meeting
       by greeting those present and emphasizing the FMI’s ongoing modernization, a Federation that looks to the future while respecting the sport’s founding values.
      Giacomo Agostini, 15-time World Champion, a rider and figure who represents the history of motorcycling, the present, and an inspiration for new generations, then
       spoke. The restyling of the FMI logo was conceived and created by designer Aldo Drudi, who delivered a video message. The conference was also attended by TC
        President Alberto Bonacossa, Elena Buffa di Perrero, and Director Martina Alabiso. The new symbol stands out for its easier reading of the FMI acronym and its
        elegant yet modern colors. It will be used during the 2025 FMI Membership Campaign and on all new Italian Motorcycling Federation materials.

FMI President Giovanni Copioli Confirmed as FIM Vice President and FIM Awards

On Saturday, 7 December, in Palma de Mallorca, during the first Board of Directors meeting following the FIM General Assembly, the President of the Italian
Motorcycling Federation (FMI), Giovanni Copioli, was confirmed as Vice President of the Fédération Internationale de Motocyclisme (FIM). The appointment,
which Copioli first received in 2022, further acknowledges the international significance of Italian motorcycling.
The FIM Board of Directors is composed of President Jorge Viegas; Vice Presidents Ignacio Verneda (Vicar) and Giovanni Copioli; members Abdulrahman Al Mannai,
Hakan Leeman, Giedré Leskauskiene, and Sebastien Poirier; and representatives of the Continental Unions elected by their assemblies: Marco Comana (FIM Africa),
Stephan Carapiet (FIM Asia), Michal Sikora (FIM Europe), Pedro Venturo Jr. (FIM Latin America), Robert Dingman (FIM North America), and Peter Doyle (FIM Oceania).
Giovanni Copioli was also appointed President of the FIM Finance Committee, of which he was already a member.
On the evening of Saturday, 7 December, FMI President Copioli attended the FIM Awards, the ceremony celebrating the World Champions. During the event, he
awarded the 2024 Enduro World Champions, including Andrea Verona (E2) and Manuel Verzeroli (EY). Earlier, during the General Assembly on 6th of December,
Moreno Crestale, winner of the FIM Touring World Challenge (the Motor Touring World Championship), had also been recognized.

## Pagina 50

FMI National Elective Assembly

      On Sunday, 15 December 2024, the 76th edition of the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) Ordinary Elective National Assembly took place in Riccione. Giovanni
        Copioli was confirmed as President of the Italian Motorcycling Federation. President Copioli was re-elected with 8,138 votes, representing 96.8% of the total,
       marking his third consecutive term at the helm of FMI for the 2025–2028 four-year period.
        At the conclusion of the Assembly, President Copioli stated: “I am truly very moved to have achieved this result. The goal will continue to be to never stop and to
        look toward ever-new challenges to further grow Motorcycling. This result can only be achieved through teamwork, which is why I am also deeply satisfied with the
        election of the new Federal Management Council, with whom I will share this third term, full of intense commitment and passionate work. I sincerely thank all those
      who have placed their trust in me and in all the members of the Federal Management Council. I feel honoured to represent and lead, for another four years, what for
     me is the great family of the Italian Motorcycling Federation.”
       During the Assembly, video greetings were shared by prominent figures, including CONI President Giovanni Malagò and FIM President Jorge Viegas.
       The ten members of the Federal Management Council were elected as follows:
       •   Elected from Affiliates: Giuseppe Bartolucci, Rocco Lopardo, Letizia Marchetti, Francesco Mezzasalma, Lorena Sangiorgi, Armando Sponga, Mario Volpe
       •   Elected from Licensed Members: Alessandro Maccioni, Francesca Nocera
       •   Elected from Trainers: Raffaele Prisco
       Francesco Brandi was confirmed as President of the Board of Auditors.

FMI Moto d’Oro. The Grand Event Celebrating Champions and Key Figures of Italian Motorcycling

On Sunday, 15 December, the day of the FMI Elective Assembly, the institutional event was made even more memorable and prestigious by the Moto d’Oro FMI
celebration.
The first edition, presented by Rachele Sangiuliano and Marco Masetti, featured numerous figures from the motorcycling world at the PalaRiccione, who distinguished
themselves through their history, dedication, and passion, moving the hundreds of attendees present in the hall.
President Copioli presented the first Moto d’Oro – whose design was created by Aldo Drudi – to Giacomo Agostini. Subsequently, CONI Vice President Vicario Silvia
Salis awarded a Moto d’Oro to the Federal President Giovanni Copioli.
Davide Tardozzi received the Moto Ingenium award on behalf of Ducati. World Champions Andrea Verona (E2 class) and Manuel Verzeroli (EY class) were awarded
the Sports Champion of the Year prize. The Moto Club Bergamo received the Sports Moto Club of the Year award.
FMI also granted further recognitions to Romano Bernasconi (A Life for Motorcycling), Filippo Rovelli (Fair Play), the TV program Dreams Road with hosts Emerson
Gattafoni and Valeria Cagnoni (Moto Press), the Enzo e Dino Ferrari Circuit of Imola – Formula Imola S.p.A. represented by DG Pietro Benvenuti (Sustainability),
and the non-profit Riders4Riders Association represented by Dante Badiali (Diversity and Inclusion). The Special Awards and Sports Awards were created by the
Italian company Tijey.
President Copioli also presented Silver Motos to the Federal Management Council members of the 2021–2024 term, to FMI Secretary General Alberto Rinaldelli,
and Bronze Motos to the Presidents of the Regional Committees. A commemorative plaque was awarded to the Moto Clubs celebrating 100 years of affiliation with
FMI during the 2021–2024 period: Erba, Bustese, Magenta, Fagnanese, Italo Palli, Pino Medeot, Oggiono, Padova, Bassano, Schio, Seveso, Ancona “Lattanzi” – La                                                                                                          49Casetta, Prato, Emilio Marchi 1921, and Moto Velo Club Lecco.
President FMI Giovanni Copioli concluded by thanking all attendees of the Moto d’Oro FMI, stating that they made this first edition unforgettable.
Among the various awards presented during the FMI Elective Assembly, particular importance is given here to the Special “Sustainability” Award, presented to
the Enzo e Dino Ferrari Circuit of Imola, in the presence of Director General Pietro Benvenuti, for exemplary management of environmental aspects and strong
commitment to the sustainability of the facility. According to a statement from the Circuit itself, the Imola facility “stood out for initiatives aimed at reducing
environmental impact and promoting eco-sustainable practices, representing a virtuous model for motorcycling and sport in general.”

## Pagina 54

FMI Sustainability Report 2023: “Measuring ourselves over time to act beyond
        time”

       “Measuring ourselves over time to act beyond time.” This is the slogan used for
        the 2023 Sustainability Report, available on the website www.federmoto.it and
       presented during the Plenary Meeting of the Italian Motorcycling Federation,
        held on Saturday, December 14, just hours after the 76th Ordinary National
        Elective Assembly. The plenary meeting also addressed the protocol signed
        with ANCI Città dei Motori, focusing on Tourism and Road Safety, and with
        the Carabinieri Sports Center. The discussion focused on the establishment of
        the eponymous Moto Club and recalled the milestone achieved in September
       by FMI Talento Azzurro Luca Lunetta, the  first motorcycle rider to enter the
        Carabinieri Sports Center. The practices carried out with the Italian Army were
        also mentioned, laying the foundation for a series of joint initiatives between the
       two institutions. The nineteenth edition of the reporting document considers the
        material aspects, shared with stakeholders and analyzed from a social, economic,
        environmental, and sporting perspective, which have had a significant impact52       on the FMI’s activities and significant implications for the world of motorcycling,
       both competitive and otherwise. Furthermore, it highlights the processes, results,
       and impacts generated by the FMI, integrating the accounting data present in
        the financial statements with information regarding strategic objectives and the
       methods used to achieve them throughout the year. Among the new features of
         this edition is the introduction of the 231 organizational model, which is located
        within the Identity and Governance section. The Sustainability Report is divided
         into seven sections: Introduction, Identity and Governance, Social Performance,
        Sporting Performance, Economic Performance, Environmental Performance, and
        Sustainable Improvement. To facilitate the document’s reading, the Methodological
       Note is of particular importance.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

Collari d’Oro 2024: Highest Honor for President Giovanni Copioli and Andrea Verona

This prestigious recognition is usually awarded to athletes who, during the season, represented Italy by achieving major international results. This time, however, the
award was also granted to President Giovanni Copioli for his international achievements. Copioli was recently reconfirmed as Vice President of the International
Motorcycling Federation.
CONI also awarded the prestigious honor to Andrea Verona (Enduro World Champion, E2 class). Accompanied by President Copioli, Verona received the award from
the hands of CONI President Giovanni Malagò during the ceremony held in the Sinopoli Hall of the Auditorium Parco della Musica in Rome. For Andrea Verona, this
is his second Collare d’Oro, following the award in 2022 for winning the absolute title in the EnduroGP World Championship. Verona’s record also includes three
consecutive E1 World Championships, the 2021 Six Days, and numerous youth titles, including the 2018 Junior Trophy of the Six Days.
Regarding President Copioli, he was also conferred the honor of Commander of the Order of Merit of the Italian Republic, received in Riccione, which further
testifies to his longstanding commitment and the importance of his work over the years.

Social performance

## Pagina 58

Foreword                         Stakeholders play a key role in the life of every organization. This centrality makes them privileged interlocutors of the FMI
                                                            itself, which is required to assess their needs, expectations, and objectives, by virtue of the relationship of exchange and
                                        mutual influence that each stakeholder establishes with its parent organization.

                                                  In honor of this relationship, Federmoto has long since initiated a process for identifying and mapping stakeholders, followed
                                     by an assessment of their expectations. For 2024, the classification criteria adopted for the 2018/2019 Sustainability Report
                                            are confirmed, starting from a defined and consolidated baseline over the past years and then articulated in an updated
                                    mapping of the Federation’s various stakeholders.

                                          Furthermore,  it is worth noting that a thorough assessment of each stakeholder was carried out, starting with their level
                                               of interest and influence on the FMI. The outcome of this process was highlighted in the “matrix,” proving to be a useful
                                               indicator for guiding the Federation’s strategic management in relation to the various stakeholders involved and influential in
                                           the decisions and actions implemented by Federmoto during the year under review and with a view to future strategies.

       Stakeholder mapping            The identification and mapping of stakeholders, which according to UNI ISO 26000 represent a crucial first phase of the Social
                                             Responsibility journey, were carried out starting from the FMI’s strategic objectives, broken down into various specific aspects.
56                                                  Specifically, they arose from the analysis of institutional documents (previous social reports, strategic plans, website, materials
                                    and internal reporting, etc.) and from the internal involvement of federal governance and FMI staff. To facilitate identification,
                               we considered the fact that some stakeholders are internal while others are external to the FMI, keeping in mind that all are
                                                   sufficiently representative and credible.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 62

Diffusion of motorcycling        Members
        practice
                                        For 2024, the Federation counts 105,545 license holders, regularly distributed across the national territory (slightly lower
                                    compared to 2023).

                                         License holders are consistently spread throughout the country and have access to membership services, as well as the
                                           opportunity to participate in all sporting and non-sporting events organized by the Federation through its affiliated Moto Clubs.

                                                        All FMI license holders share a passion for two-wheeled vehicles, but their individual needs vary. The FMI is committed to
                                             creating a unified and cohesive group to provide a wider range of services that are useful, efficient, and rewarding for those
                                who receive them. FMI license holders can be divided into those who practice Sports (including holders of a sports license)
                                    and those who simply cultivate their passion for two wheels in all its facets.

                                         Services for Members

                                         For years, the Italian Motorcycling Federation has been committed to offering increasingly innovative services, developing
                                                effective and cutting-edge solutions that satisfy its members.60
                                Member Cards: 98.088 (-3,3% with respect to the 2023 period)
                                                          •   Free roadside assistance (towing of the motorcycle in case of breakdown or accident, expenses in case of lost keys,
                                         expenses in case of lost documents, repatriation of the motorcycle, and much more);
                                                          •   Free healthcare assistance (ambulance transport, 24/7 telephone support);
                                                          •   Discounts on admission tickets to the most important international and national sporting events held in Italy;
                                                          •   Discounts on admission tickets to the most important motorcycle trade shows and exhibitions organized in Italy;
                                                          •   Participation in contests and various initiatives;
                                                          •   Discounts on motorcycle insurance;
                                                          •   Discounts on accessories and apparel.
                                        Sport Cards: 7.050 (-2,2% with respect to the 2023 period)
                                                          •    All benefits included with the Member Card;
                                                          •   Free practice sessions on FMI-approved tracks;
                                                          •    Circuit Racing [AMM17.1][GP17.2]in Off-Road disciplines (Motocross, Enduro, Trial, Speedway, Snowmobiles, Quad,
                                                   Motorally, Supermoto) at all approved facilities;
                                                          •   Promotional events at the local level organized by Regional Committees, as regulated by national and regional Sports

Mini-Sport Cards: 407 (-23,2% with respect to the 2023 period)
The Mini-Sport Card was introduced in 2020 and is reserved for children aged between 6 and 9 years old.
•   Training on Minimotodromes (Circuit racing disciplines)
•   Training on approved test tracks, Easy and Baby Cross tracks, Enduro and Trial areas, and, from age 8 onwards, on FMI-

•   Accident and third-party liability (RCT) insurance coverage
•    All benefits included with the Member Card

Motorcycle Clubs               1.698 (-4,4% with respect to the 2023 period)
                                  For several years, the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FIM) has been supporting Motorcycle Clubs, which play a vital role in the
                            management of motorcycling and all related activities, especially at the local level. The FIM manages direct contact with local
                                        authorities, which is essential to enabling all activities. It also organizes local activities mandated by the Regional Committees.

                              The Federation is committed to researching and improving (both qualitatively and quantitatively) the tools needed to establish
                                       direct contact with Motorcycle Clubs (a dedicated federal mailbox for each FIM and a monthly newsletter with initiatives and
                                     regulatory updates).

                                            Finally, the FIM provides enthusiasts with all the necessary information on the services offered and organizes motorcycle
                                            activities, coordinated by the FMI, in which all members are welcome to participate.

## Pagina 80

Training                       The activity of the Training Commission in 2024 focused on continuing the commitments undertaken for the implementation of
                                           the 2020–2024 four-year program. The objective was to create a homogeneous and widespread training system using a shared
                                        methodology, designed to foster the development and professional growth of the roles within the Federation’s structure, both
                                                at the territorial and national level. The Commission aimed to provide skills to anticipate and address social, economic, and
                                          environmental changes, to implement innovation processes, and to establish a system for the recognition and certification of
                                        competencies.
                                                  In line with the defined training pathways for each role within FMI, courses were organized for sports managers operating at
                                          the national level (Race Directors, Race Commissioners, etc.) and at the territorial level (Regional Representatives of the
                                 Women’s Commission, Sports Managers of Moto Clubs and COREs). Teaching was delivered in blended mode, both online
                                    and in person.
                                                  In addition, three courses for national and territorial managers were designed and produced, delivered in e-learning format:

                                         •  Development of Thinking and Argumentation – 10 video lessons, each lasting 20 minutes
                                         •   Meeting Management – 5 video lessons, each lasting 20 minutes
78                                      •  Time Management – 3 video lessons, each lasting 20 minutes
      Communication                     In 2024, the Communications Office was merged with the Marketing Office to provide greater scope for activities focused
                                    on the creation and dissemination of content for both the federal organization—defined as the National, central, and local
                                               structures, Motorcycle Clubs, and Members—and for external users, particularly the media.

                                    The Marketing and Communications Office ensures reliability and timeliness in both internal and external communications to
                                           the Federation. The operational methodology requires a well-defined organization of much of the work performed; at the same
                                              time, flexibility and availability are required to respond quickly to current events and breaking news.

                                    The objective of FMI Communications is, through its projects and initiatives, to raise awareness of the Italian Motorcycling
                                          Federation among all users, both from a sporting and institutional perspective.

                                       at www.motitalia.it. Annual issues of the magazine are distributed to motorcycle clubs, manufacturers, businesses, institutions,

                                     to publish some articles. It has a broad audience, appealing to both sports enthusiasts and those who use motorcycles in
                              numerous ways. In 2024, the column dedicated to the Women’s Commission continued, and Radio Box, a behind-the-scenes
                                   look at the Italian Circuit Racing Championships, was added. Within the digital edition, each piece of content offers the option
                                      of viewing videos, photo galleries, and external websites.

The primary objective, however, remains to reach Members and motorcycle enthusiasts through a wealth of information
disseminated through its own communication channels (online and offline).

The FMI, in fact, is an organization attentive to the needs of motorcyclists: the Member (in a broader sense, the motorcyclist)
is the cornerstone of its development model. This strategic choice defines the FMI’s objective: to respond to the needs of
motorcyclists by contributing to the realization of their dreams. User satisfaction with the quality of the services offered is,
therefore, a critical success factor that qualifies the tactical and strategic actions undertaken by the FMI.
