Road safety education         The FMI has included, under Article 3 of its Statute, the protection of road motorcycling users. In this regard, one of its primary
                                    commitments is to educate young people in the correct use of two-wheeled vehicles.

                                      Over time, the Federation has established itself as an organization dedicated to promoting road safety as a fundamental
                                                 cultural factor for civil coexistence. For this reason, the FMI participates directly in working groups aimed at increasing the
                                      awareness necessary for safe driving and, more broadly, for the correct use of the road environment.
                                      Drawing on its strong track record in track riding, the FMI aims to raise awareness among young people about the safe
                                         handling of motorized two-wheelers, through practical training sessions conducted by highly qualified instructors, in traffic-
                                                free protected areas, and covered by an insurance plan provided directly by the FMI. A preliminary stage of these courses
                                            includes awareness-raising activities on road safety, the use of protective equipment, and the correct interpretation of the
                                          “road” environment. These experiences, mainly aimed at young people, are offered free of charge within schools.

                                               Practical riding tests may be carried out directly at schools following theoretical courses dedicated to compliance with rules
                                    and the use of protective equipment, or they may be organized within the framework of fairs and public events, thus involving
                                      a larger number of students from different schools.
88
                                    The FMI operates in schools thanks to a consolidated collaboration with the Ministry of Education, with which it is committed
                                              to organizing training courses on road safety education. With the goal of raising awareness of the road environment among
                                            children and young people, the FMI provides theoretical road safety courses within schools, complemented, where possible,
                                     by practical safe-riding sessions on mopeds (for younger children, the practical component is carried out on bicycles).

                                     These courses are aimed at children and young people of all ages, with particular attention to those approaching or having just
                                          turned 14. The training is delivered by FMI-certified road safety instructors, who have themselves received specific training
                                         through a program agreed with the Ministry of Education, enabling them to officially teach road safety as part of the school
                                             curriculum.

                                    The year 2024 was characterized by the following road safety education projects:

                                                          •   The close cooperation with the Ministry of Infrastructure and Transport led to the organization of educational events within
                                               the Civil Motorization Offices of Novara, Domodossola, Vercelli, Rome, and Florence.
                                                          •   Continued collaboration between FMI and the Ministry of Education and Merit on the Edustrada.it platform, through the
                                                 projects “Mettiamo in moto la sicurezza” (Let’s Put Safety in Motion), “Piccole ruote crescono” (Little Wheels Grow), and
                                           “E vissero tutti sicuri e contenti” (And They All Lived Safe and Sound).

Motorcycling volunteering       Volunteer Motorcyclists, with their experience and ability to navigate even in remote areas, are able to quickly reach disaster-
                                      stricken areas, even in difficult-to-access conditions. Through reconnaissance and spotting activities, they provide crucial
                                     information to emergency teams, enabling a timely and targeted response. They intervene to search for missing persons,
                                        deliver aid to the area, report the presence of waste and hazardous substances, and spot early signs of fires.
                              The FMI was included in the Register of Civil Protection Volunteer Associations by the Civil Protection Department of the
                                  Presidency of the Council of Ministers in 2016 and can count on the operations of 212 Volunteer Motorcyclists, ready to
                                respond in the event of a disaster.
                                          In September 2023, the Veneto Region included the Federation in the Regional Civil Protection Register. Fifteen volunteers
                                     are currently trained to be activated in the event of disasters in the region, and in the summer of 2024, they conducted their
                                                   first exercise. On that occasion, the motorcyclist volunteers simulated reconnaissance and spotting operations in wooded
                                   areas and remote areas, practicing patrolling procedures, reporting fire outbreaks, and gathering useful information for the
                                  Operations Room. The exercise tested reaction times, radio communications, and the ability to safely cover a large area,
                                    confirming the importance of having agile and fast units like motorcycles in the field.
                              The Carabinieri Forestali, with whom the FMI has signed a Memorandum of Understanding, also rely on the collaboration of
                                     motorcyclist volunteers. In the summer of 2024, FMI motorcyclists conducted reconnaissance of the area under the supervision
                                       of the Civil Protection and the Carabinieri Forestali, providing generous support in control and reconnaissance activities to
                                  promptly report the outbreak of fires and provide assistance to the population. Activated by the National Department of Civil
                                      Protection, the FMI joined the Forest Fire Prevention Campaign through special twinning agreements with the Calabria and
                                    Lazio regions.
                               Thanks to these agreements, teams of our volunteer motorcyclists travelled through the areas of Lamezia Terme, Cosenza,
                              and Pollino in Calabria, along routes defined based on a mapping of the most critical points provided by the Civil Protection,
                                            in agreement with the Calabria Verde Operations Room, from the sea to the mountains, to report the presence of fires.
                                      Traveling on unpaved roads by motorcycle allowed them to cover a large area, passing through potentially dangerous areas
                                     several times throughout the day, clearly visible, thus acting as a deterrent to potential malicious acts. The reconnaissance also
                                  allowed our volunteers to raise awareness among forest users about keeping their areas clean.
                                          In Campania, too, the Provincial Command of the Carabinieri Forestali of Salerno called upon the FMI, along with all the other
                                    associations that regularly contribute to forest fire prevention and spotting. This synergistic activity, in what until two years ago
                            was considered the area with the highest number of malicious fires in Italy, has led to a progressive reduction in fires, so much
                                so that the number of reports from our volunteers to the Carabinieri Forestali has decreased by 70 percent and the number of
                                             fires started has decreased by 80%.
                              The model of territorial control with high-visibility patrols has also been proposed for 2024, prioritizing deterrent activity
                                     with small teams of motorcyclists during hours and days designated as high-risk. If a fire is spotted, the volunteer sends a
                                  georeferenced photograph, allowing immediate location by airborne vehicles.

Public Administration          As part of the interest representation activity carried out during 2024, the following meetings took place:

                             The Italian Motorcycling Federation in Parliament in support of the Parliamentary Intergroup for Motorcycle Mobility
                                         In 2024, the FMI continued its work in collaboration with the Parliamentary Intergroup for Motorcycle Mobility to promote
                                          legislative initiatives and implement promotional proposals that promote two-wheeled mobility and road safety, improve rider
                                        safety, support production, and promote motorcycle racing. The intergroup, founded in 2023, brings together parliamentarians
                          who are enthusiastic about motorcycles and are therefore aware of the issues facing this sector. It was established at the
                                             initiative of Senator Erika Stefani and its members include Senators Gian Marco Centinaio (Vice President of the Senate),
                                   Gianni Berrino, Massimo Garavaglia (Chairman of the Finance Committee), Guido Quintino Liris, Stefania Pucciarelli, Domenica
                                           Spinelli, Giusy Versace, and the Honorables Roberto Bagnasco, Dario Giagoni, Giandonato La Salandra, and Ciro Maschio.

                                   Motorcyclists are vulnerable road users and 125cc motorcycles can travel on motorways and ring roads
                                                          It was precisely thanks to the support of the Parliamentary Intergroup that the FMI, together with ANCMA (the National
                                   Association of Motorcycle Manufacturers), achieved two major milestones. In 2024, motorcyclists were officially recognized
                                as vulnerable road users, and 125cc motorcycles were granted access to highways and ring roads. The approval of this
                                         legislation was preceded by a Senate Hearing with the FMI President, who advocated in support of the requests submitted,
                                as well as by FMI’s participation in a working group on the application of Motorcycle Protection Devices (DSM) to guardrails.   93
                              Below are the most significant amendments to the Highway Code requested and obtained by FMI in 2024 to enhance
                                    motorcyclist safety:
                                               •       recognizing motorcyclists as vulnerable road users;
                                               •      work on crash barriers, with the installation of the third “motorcyclist safety” (DSM) category;
                                               •      access to highways and main extra-urban roads for motorcycles with an engine capacity greater than 120 cc and
                                     greater than 6 kW if electric, provided they are ridden by adults;
                                               •      commitment to promoting culture within schools, including through extracurricular road safety courses;
                                               •       protection of vehicles of historical and collectible interest;
                                               •       permission for motorcycles to tow a trailer.

                            FMI obtains permission for historical vehicles to circulate in Rome
                                         In 2024, with Rome Mayoral Ordinance No. 122 of October 30, 2024, it was finally established that vehicles of historical and
                                        collectible interest, along with mopeds over thirty years old, could circulate within the Green Belt, in derogation of the anti-
                                       pollution bans in force.
                                       After many battles fought at the Municipality, the Lazio Region, and in court (Regional Administrative Court and the Council
                                      of State), the exemption from the circulation ban was granted, until October 31, 2025, but with good hopes that it will be
                                 confirmed in the future as well. This exemption applies to motorcycles of historical and collectible interest registered in the

## Pagina 96

historical registers (as per Article 60 of the New Highway Code) and to mopeds over thirty years old, with a certificate of
                                                  historical and collectible importance.
                                    The Municipal Ordinance was issued following Lazio Region Resolution No. 122 of October 10, 2024. 782, which established
                                               “that the restrictions on vehicular traffic [...] do not apply to vehicles of historical and collectible interest pursuant to Article 60
                                               of Legislative Decree No. 285/1992 and subsequent amendments (New Highway Code) and to mopeds over thirty years old
                                            with a certificate of historical relevance.”
                                            This follows the Lazio Regional Administrative Court’s ruling 15408/2023, which upheld the appeal filed by the historical
                                                registers against the ban on the circulation of vehicles of historical and collectible interest enacted by the Municipality of Rome
                                                    in 2022.
                                            This is the result of a dispute initiated in 2022 with the Municipality of Rome, against which the FMI reiterated the need to
                                             generally encourage two-wheeled mobility, including that of vehicles recognized as being of historical and collectible interest,
                                       which have a low environmental impact given their limited number compared to the total circulating fleet. These have significant
                                                  historical and cultural value and generate significant economic benefits for the area. Thanks to their smaller size, shorter travel
                                             times, and fewer traffic jams, two-wheelers contribute to reducing city traffic.

                                             Carabinieri Sports Centre Athlete: Rider Luca Lunetta
94                                              In 2024, Pata Talento Azzurro FMI rider Luca Lunetta became the first motorcyclist to join the Carabinieri Sports Centre. The
                                   Moto3 World Championship rider, competing with the SIC 58 team, officially took the oath on 26 November at the Carabinieri
                                               Training School in Turin. This milestone marked Lunetta’s formal entry into the Carabinieri Sports Centre, paving the way for
                                            other motorcycle racers to follow.
                                    The agreement was made possible thanks to the close collaboration between the Carabinieri and the Italian Motorcycling
                                          Federation (FMI), which continues to be deeply committed to nurturing and supporting young talents. Lunetta’s appointment as
                                           the first rider of the Carabinieri Sports Centre was announced during a well-attended press conference held in early September
                                                at Misano, alongside the San Marino and Riviera di Rimini Grand Prix.

                                   The FIM Rally Receives Patronage from the Ministry of Tourism
                                    The FIM Rally, the most prestigious international motorcycle touring event, held in Chianciano Terme (SI) from 26 to 28 June
                                        2024, received official patronage from the Italian Ministry of Tourism and was granted support from the National Tourism Fund
                                          (FUNT).
                                            This prestigious recognition highlighted the importance of motorcycle tourism for Italy, a country that hosts numerous events
                                         across its territory—initiatives that promote a culture of sustainable and environmentally responsible motorcycle travel. These
                                         events also generate significant economic benefits for local communities and offer participants the opportunity to discover
                                          areas that remain off the beaten tourist paths, thanks also to the collaboration and support of local administrations.
                                    The Ministry of Tourism’s growing attention to the needs of motorcyclists, as represented by the FMI, demonstrates a shared

commitment to developing specialized services aimed at enhancing each region’s tourism offer and promoting  it on the

Memorandum of Understanding with ANCI Motori
In 2024, the FMI renewed its Memorandum of Understanding with ANCI Motori, a branch of the National Association of Italian
Municipalities (ANCI) that brings together municipalities with a strong connection to the motor industry.

The Memorandum establishes collaboration in the following areas:
· Road safety and traffic education
· Promotion of motorcycle tourism
· Enhancement and protection of Italy’s historic and vintage motorcycle heritage
· Off-road riding
· Urban mobility

Collaboration with the Ministry of Infrastructure and Transport
In 2024, cooperation with the Ministry of Infrastructure and Transport continued on topics related to road circulation, the
recognition of vehicles of historic and collectible interest, and joint road safety education projects.                     95
Edustrada.it and collaboration with the Ministry of Education and Merit
The collaboration with the Ministry of Education and Merit also continued uninterrupted, aimed at expanding road safety
education in schools through both theoretical and practical activities. These initiatives focus on risk awareness and the safe
use of motorized two-wheel vehicles. The road safety education projects “Piccole ruote crescono,” “E vissero sicuri e contenti,”
and “Mettiamo in moto la sicurezza,” targeting primary and secondary schools, are delivered through the Ministry’s digital
platform Edustrada.it.

## Pagina 98

The Historical Register           The Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) has established the National Historical Register to protect the national motoring
                                             heritage and promote research, restoration, and conservation of motorcycles. Pursuant to Article 3, paragraph 2 of the Federal
                                            Regulations, the Federal Management Council has established the Historical Register Commission, which is responsible for:
                                ●      promoting research, restoration, and conservation of motorcycles of historical interest;
                                ●      promoting historical-cultural events such as conferences, exhibitions, shows, etc.;
                                ●       creating projects and initiatives dedicated to the valorization of vintage vehicles;
                                ●       registering the motorcycle heritage present in the country.


                                   The FMI Historical Register is recognized by the Highway Code and is among the registers authorized by Ministerial
                                         Decree of December 17, 2009 (published in the Official Gazette 55 of March 18, 2010) to issue the Certificate of
                                                    Historical and Collectible Significance (CRS), which qualifies the vehicle as a vehicle of historical and collectible interest.
                                                Registration in the Historical Register in some regions allows for discounted road tax payments and, pursuant to Article
                                       152 of the Highway Code, also exempts vehicles from the requirement to drive with daytime running lights on.
                                         Following the complete digitalization of registration procedures and services for motorcycles already listed in the96                                                   Historic Register—accessible exclusively through the myFMI portal, which allows users to monitor the progress of
                                                       their applications in real time—the Historic Register has also become a comprehensive digital archive containing all
                                            motorcycles for which registration has been granted.


                                                          •  A major innovation introduced in 2024 for motorcycle enthusiasts who own a vehicle registered in the Historic Register was
                                               the release of an exclusive package that includes the book “Metalli Preziosi” (not available for sale), a folder containing
                                               the printed Certificate of Historical and Collectible Relevance, a photograph of the registered motorcycle, a limited-edition
                                           motorcycle-themed postage stamp issued by Poste Italiane, and a rare illustrated postcard.

                                        There are two types of procedures for applying for registration in the Historic Register and for obtaining the Certificate of
                                                Historical and Collectible Relevance.
                                                          •   Procedure “A” is reserved for motorcycles that are roadworthy and compliant with circulation regulations.
                                                          •   Procedure “B” applies to motorcycles that are not roadworthy (e.g., decommissioned, deregistered, or of unknown origin).
                                         For motorcycles used exclusively in competitions (motocross and circuit racing), it is also possible to request the issuance of
                                      a Certificate of Historical and Technical Conformity.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 100

Motorcycle Tourism            The traditional definition of Federal Motorcycle Tourism as a form of tourism practiced by traveling on a motorcycle is now
                                         considered too limited. FMI members share a deep passion for motorcycles—not only as a means of transportation but, above
                                                                all, as a lifestyle: one that is dynamic, curious, and open to discovering lesser-known destinations.
                               A new and strong environmental awareness has emerged, along with a growing sense of responsibility in actively contributing
                                              to the promotion of local tourism. Within the Federation, efforts have been made to identify evolving needs and expectations
                                              to further enhance the overall experience, while maintaining the core values of quality, safety, and environmental respect.
                                         For members, motorcycle tourism represents a valuable experiential opportunity. The number of events continues to grow,
                                       as does the overall quality of the offerings. Clubs are increasingly aware of the sector’s potential and are introducing renewed
                                              organizational models and innovative event formats capable of attracting new participants. Efforts have also been strengthened
                                                    in the field of sustainability, with awareness initiatives aimed at organizing events that increasingly align with the Federation’s
                                             values.

                                   FMI Moto Tourism App
                               A new and practical tool has been made available to members—a fast and convenient way to register attendance at events and
                                              activate the relevant insurance policy. It also provides organizers with an effective system for monitoring participant numbers.
98                                 The Trophy of the Regions
                                    The Trophy of the Regions is the most prestigious and popular gathering for motorcycle tourism enthusiasts. Established and
                                     promoted by the FMI Tourism Commission, it was created to offer an opportunity for community and high-quality entertainment.
                                    Each year, it attracts thousands of members from every Italian region.
                                                  In 2024, the event received official recognition and patronage from the Italian Ministry of Tourism.
                                    The 2024 edition, the sixth in the history of the FMI, took place in Sicily from Friday, September 27 to Sunday, September
                                            29. The 1,500 participants, representing their respective Regional Committees, explored the southeastern part of the island,
                                          covering hundreds of kilometres on their motorcycles. The sea, inland areas, hidden territories, picturesque landscapes, and
                                        well-known locations all served as the backdrop for this major event.
                                    The event began on Friday, September 27, with registrations at the base camp—the Mangia’s Resort in Brucoli (SR)—followed
                                     by a musical evening in Augusta (SR). On Saturday, each participant had the opportunity to discover Sicily by taking part in one
                                               of the organized routes. There were twelve destinations: Vizzini, Caltagirone, Canicattini Bagni, Noto, Ispica, Modica, Scicli,
                                           the Ragusa coastal road, Ragusa, Donnafugata, Chiaramonte Gulfi, and Catania. Among the intermediate stops were Avola,
                                     Marzamemi, Ragusa Ibla, Nicolosi (a few kilometres from Mount Etna), and Rosolini.
                                                  In the evening, the traditional “Evening of the Regions” took place at Porto Xiphonio in Augusta—a journey through the flavours
                                    and culinary traditions of Italy, thanks to the Regional Committees, which presented high-quality food and wine in a festive
                                        atmosphere.
                                    The evening’s entertainment was preceded by greetings from the Mayor of Augusta, Giuseppe Di Mare; the FMI President,





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

Regional Committee, Salvatore Di Pace; and Marco Caruso, Secretary of the Regional Committee and organizer of the Trophy.

## Pagina 102

FMI Camping Trophy
                                A new type of event, the FMI Camping Trophy, was established and promoted by the Tourism Commission in September
                                         2024. Its goal is to encourage meetings and gatherings among motorcycle tourism enthusiasts in a camping format. This new
                                           entertainment proposal offers programs of high tourist and cultural interest within the host region and fosters tourism and
                                             sporting exchange among participants.

                                               Historical Reenactments of Urban Circuits
                                      Urban circuits once played a major role in Italy’s motorsport history. Nearly every city had its own track, and races were held
                                         almost every Sunday, until 1957, when, following another tragic event (the Mille Miglia car race), they were banned. Today,
                                          these circuits are being revived in a historical and touristic key, inspiring the rediscovery and documentation of these urban
                                          routes—authentic pieces of Italian motorcycling heritage.
                                     The activity had been facing organizational challenges, but since 2022, when management was transferred from the Sport
                                            Heritage Sector to the Tourism Sector, along with the introduction of new regulations better suited to enthusiasts’ needs, the
                                                       field has experienced a strong revival.

                                   FIM Rally
100                                 The FIM Rally is the annual gathering organized by the Fédération Internationale de Motocyclisme (FIM). It is regarded as the
                                     most important event in the international motorcycle tourism calendar, attracting thousands of enthusiasts from around the
                                          world each year.
                                     The first edition took place in Berlin in 1936 during the Olympic Games, with participants from 11 countries. Estonia won that
                                             inaugural edition, while Italy claimed its first title in Groningen (Netherlands) in 1950, during the first post-war edition.
                                     The Italian Federation accepted the challenge of organizing the 77th edition of the FIM Rally in Chianciano Terme.
                                                                        It was an event rich in experiences—an authentic journey through medieval villages, culture, and gastronomy. Held from June
                                     26 to 28, 2024, the rally welcomed over 1,000 motorcyclists from 27 countries. Participants had the opportunity to discover
                                            the most hidden and evocative corners of the province of Siena, as well as some of the most famous Umbrian art cities in Italy.
                                     The event was designed around the principles of sustainability, introducing a new organizational model that enabled the host
                                             region not only to welcome the event but also to share in its values and benefits. (For further details on the sustainability of
                                    FMI events, see page 150.

                                          Celebration of the FMI Motorcycle Tourism Champions 2024 in Atri
                                A day dedicated to celebrating the passion for two wheels, the joy of community, and the spirit of adventure and discovery
                                                that defines FMI Moto Clubs and members. The 2024 FMI Motorcycle Tourism Champions Award Ceremony took place on
                                           Saturday, November 30, at the Municipal Theatre of Atri (TE).
                                     The event was attended by FMI Vice President and Coordinator of the Tourism Commission Rocco Lopardo, President of the

Technical Working Group       The Safety Commission participated in the Technical Working Group established by the Ministry of Transport to assess possible
on DSM Guard Rails           amendments to the 2019 Decree on the DSM Motorcycle Safety Barrier System. The Commission presented the perspective

## Pagina 104

Accident Analysis During       As in previous four-year cycles, the collection and analysis of accident data continued. The results were shared with discipline
       Events                             coordinators, with a view to future technical meetings scheduled for early 2025. These meetings will focus on the potential
                                           extension of airbag use across all motorcycle disciplines.
                                         Since 2023, mechanically activated airbags have been permitted in Motocross (MX), Mini Road (MR), and Supermoto. In road
                                                racing, starting in 2024, the use of electronically triggered airbags became mandatory for all categories.

                                     The upcoming technical meetings, organized in cooperation with manufacturers, will provide a reference framework for
                                        Category 1, 2, and 3 airbags, in accordance with FIM Motocross Technical Rules, and define suitability for each FMI discipline.

                                   By continuing accident analysis during races, starting next season,  it will be possible to evaluate the impact of Level 2
                                             protection in Motocross and Supermoto, as well as the mandatory adoption of airbags across all road racing categories.
                                      Updates have been made regarding the certifications valid through the 2024 sporting season. Each discipline has revised its
                                                regulations, updating the tables detailing approved protective gear and corresponding certifications.

                                A particularly important advancement in terms of safety was achieved through collaboration with the Facilities Committee for
                                            the implementation of the Regulations on Non-Competitive Activities (Training) at homologated facilities. Training sessions,
102                                   even individual ones, are now subject to specific regulations, primarily extending to these activities the same requirements for
                                              clothing and signalling as those applied in official competitions.
                                             This regulation, together with the mandatory presence of a Track Supervisor, represents an additional step forward in raising
                                              safety standards.
                                                                        It is important to highlight the collective effort carried out in close collaboration with the Facilities Committee, the Group of
                                   Race Officials, the Training Commission, and the Licensing Office.

      The Women’s Commission        In 2024, the Women’s Commission actively promoted projects across technical, educational, and recreational areas. A key
                                         element of its work was the organization of women’s technical training courses in all motorcycling disciplines.
                                     The season concluded with a multidisciplinary training event involving Enduro, Motocross, and Trial. The Commission also
                                          took part in the FIM Rally, an international touring event, where it organized a conference focused on women in motorcycle
                                             tourism, aiming to celebrate their participation and challenge gender stereotypes.
                                     The Lady Beta FMI project continued successfully, featuring the participation of nine female riders competing in the Enduro
                                         Trophy and the Motorally Championship. In addition, a new “dirt bike” project was launched in the e-bike category, with the
                                     Commission covering the registration fees for all participants. Thanks to agreements with teams and manufacturers, the riders
                                      were able to participate completely free of charge.

SECTION 2 | Social performance



EMove R Group, the national distributor of Swm Binsen—a company specializing in electric off-road motorcycles since 2020
and strongly committed to supporting women in motorcycling.
The initiative provides female participants of the FMI off-road women’s training sessions with a Swm Binsen electric motorcycle
and full technical support during the three rounds of the Italian E-Bike Cross Championship, in the Dirt Bike Stock category. All
registration and management costs are fully covered by the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI).

The Swm Binsen model is particularly well-suited for women riders, thanks to its compact size and lightweight design. The
project’s main goal is to encourage greater female participation in motorcycling, while also promoting the E-Bike Cross
Championship, electric motorcycles, and a dedicated trophy organized by EMove R Group for the 2025 season.

Meet Me in Motorcycle – 3rd Edition
The Women’s Commission also organized the third edition of “Meet Me in Motorcycle”, held in October during the CIV Classic
event.
This initiative has shown significant potential, attracting not only FMI members but also newcomers—women interested
in learning to ride, joining training sessions, and connecting with other female motorcyclists. The event fosters a sense of
community, encouraging women to share their passion for motorcycles and grow together. It also contributed to an increase
in new memberships.

Participation in Trade Fairs
The Women’s Commission took part in the most important industry fairs, including Motor Bike Expo (MBE) and EICMA, to
promote female motorcycling beyond the FMI’s institutional context and further boost membership growth. At the Rome
Motorcycle Show, the Commission also organized a free introductory technical course for beginners, reinforcing its mission to
make motorcycling more accessible and inclusive for women of all experience levels.

2024 Trade Fair Events         Motor Bike Expo – Verona 2024
                                         Three days dedicated to motorcycling in all its forms. The Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) was one of the main
                                            protagonists of Motor Bike Expo, held in Verona from January 19 to 21, with activities both inside and outside its booth
                                            located in Hall 6 – Space 23D.
                                    From the very first day, visitors were invited to attend a series of engaging events. In the afternoon, at the FMI stand, a
                                        conference organized by the Women’s Commission and the Training Commission took place, focusing on motorcycling
                                    beyond the Gender Gap — a highly relevant topic within today’s industry. On this occasion, a contest was also launched,
                                                 offering a major opportunity to both female and male riders.
                               On the morning of the second day, the “Di.Di. Diversamente Disabili” Onlus presented its projects aimed at bringing young
                                       people with disabilities closer to the world of two wheels. At 11:00 a.m., the spotlight was on Riders4Riders and its initiatives
                                           supporting riders who have suffered serious motorcycle injuries. At 11:30, the “In Moto con l’Africa” team presented its
                                              projects within the CIV Classic and its fundraising campaign for Medici con l’Africa CUAMM.
                                           At 1:00 p.m., the Motorally Book was presented, offering a look back at the 2023 season. In the early afternoon, Auditorium
                                             Verdi hosted the CIV 2023 Awards Ceremony and the presentation of the 2024 Dunlop CIV. At 5:00 p.m., the day concluded at
                                            the FMI booth with the unveiling of the 2025 FMI Scrambler Calendar and the presentation of a new FMI Bikers Moto Club.
                               On the final day, the Auditorium Verdi hosted the Epoca Sport Awards (CIV Classic and Motocross), followed by the
106                                      presentation of the 2024 FMI Pata Talenti Azzurri, young athletes who represent the future of Italian motorcycling, at 3:30
                                        p.m. — an event open to all.

SECTION 2 | Social performance



EICMA – Milan-Rho 2024
At the 81st edition of EICMA, held from November 5 to 10 at Fiera Milano-Rho, the FMI celebrated Italian motorcycling with a
diverse program of events, meetings, and initiatives. The FMI was located in Hall 15, space G06.
The event opened on Tuesday, November 5, with the official inauguration of the fair. Immediately after, FMI and Ohvale
presented a joint project at the FMI booth, designed to support young riders entering the world of motorcycle racing.
On Wednesday, November 6, two key events took place at the FMI booth:
– At 12:30 p.m., a talk with Nicolò Bulega, 2023 Supersport World Champion and a standout figure in Superbike;
– At 4:30 p.m., the presentation of the 2025 Six Days of Enduro.

On Thursday, November 7 at 3:00 p.m., the first session of Safe Riding Tips was held: through FMI instructor-led videos,
visitors learned techniques on proper riding posture and control. Later, at 5:00 p.m., Alessandro Lupino shared insights from
his season that crowned him Italian Motocross Champion with Ducati.
Friday, November 8, began at 10:30 a.m. with a Mototourism segment featuring the presentation of the Rally FIM book and
the first edition of the FMI Camping Trophy. At 11:30 a.m., a talk followed with the Trial des Nations riders, who achieved
outstanding results in Maglia Azzurra.
In the afternoon, while national championship awards for Motocross, Supermoto, and E-Bike took place in Sala Martini, the
FMI booth hosted road safety training sessions for young visitors, including a simulated drunk-driving path and demonstrations   107
of protective gear. At 4:30 p.m., another Safe Riding Tips session was held.
On Saturday, November 9, at 11:00 a.m., the day began with the presentation of the Bikers’ Festival and the 2025 Mototourism
Trophy of the Regions. At 12:00 p.m. and 3:00 p.m., new road safety education sessions were conducted. At 4:30 p.m., a talk
with Luca Lunetta, Moto3 World Championship rider and FMI Pata Talento Azzurro, took place—Lunetta had already achieved
two podium finishes in his debut world season.
On the final day, Sunday, at 11:00 a.m., the Lombardy Regional Mountain Trial Trophy awards were held, followed by the
presentation of a documentary celebrating Italy’s victory at the Enduro Vintage Trophy in Camerino, with the entire team in
attendance.
At 1:00 p.m. and again at 4:00 p.m., the show concluded with additional road safety sessions, officially closing EICMA 2024
and reaffirming FMI’s commitment to safety, education, and the promotion of Italian motorcycling excellence.

The Sports Sector
 Among the activities promoted by the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) are those related to
 the Sports Sector. This sector is responsible for the promotion, management, and organization
 of motorcycling sports activities (divided into various disciplines), with the goal of improving
 the quality of athletes’ sports growth and development path. Below is an infographic-style
 description of the various disciplines, highlighting the main winners of the 2024 season:

Measuring sports                  Within the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) system, the training commission plays a strategic and cross-functional role.
       performance – identification             It is not only responsible for developing and managing athletes of all levels from a technical standpoint, but also for fostering
         of KPIs                          growth across multiple departments and for sharing the methodologies and knowledge developed internally through training
                                                     activities — which, in recent years, have become increasingly requested in a variety of contexts.

                                                In 2024, the training commission focused on the following areas:

                                                       a) Global management of disciplines under distinct aspects:
                                                                 •   Study and research on improving technical performance in relation to athletic results;
                                                                 •   Revision of working groups and partial updates to junior high-level training methodologies;
                                                                 •  Management of high-level sports activities;
                                                                 •    Territorial training activities, where the Training Directorate (DT) supports the identification of assigned Trainers or
                                                   provides consultancy on technical and methodological setups;
                                                                 •   Development of Certified Motorcycle Schools, with a new structure and enhanced operational capacity;
                                                                 •   (Non-structured) monitoring of system anomalies concerning the Training staff framework and their operational
                                            management.
114
                                                   b) Safe Riding
                                                                 •  Management of the Federal Motorcycle School (SMF) – a project aimed at motorcycle users and dedicated to road
                                                       safety riding. The SMF operated across various territories and at the Federal Training Center in Misano;
                                                                 •   Introduction of off-road safe riding among the educational programs offered to the public;
                                                                 •   Partnership agreements with scientific organizations to establish structured and specific research programs that
                                                           positively impact training content and, more broadly, the Federation’s educational mission;
                                                                 •   Implementation project to expand these activities at the Federal Training Center in Faenza.

                                                       c) Training for technical staff and other professionals
                                                                 •   1st Level Trainers;
                                                                 •   2nd Level Trainers;
                                                                 •   ITGF (Instructors of Off-Road Riding Techniques);
                                                                 •  ITGM (Instructors of Motorcycle Riding Techniques);
                                                                 •   ITGS (Instructors of Road Riding Techniques);
                                                                 •   IGAST (Instructors for Territorial Sports Activities);

## Pagina 117

SECTION 3 | Sports performance



The training activity, which represents a moment of sharing FMI’s knowledge base, holds not only a cultural role but also
a strategic one in establishing relations with companies (certified manufacturer schools and HR departments of motorcycle
brands) and institutional bodies. However, for 2024, the FMI — in agreement with the Carabinieri Police Department — decided
to suspend training activities for this year.
These external training initiatives, while showcasing the Federation’s expertise, have also opened new opportunities for
technical education aimed at external audiences. At the end of the initial three-year project cycle, the Training Directorate
renewed its educational plan for the coming years, confirming with the Carabinieri Corps the continuation of the project in
2025.

Services for the Industry
The Training Directorate, in collaboration with the multinational Brembo, designed a training program for test riders, structured
across various modules and highly appreciated by the company. Following this, a similar program was developed for Pirelli
Tires. Currently, the Training Directorate is working on new training programs requested by Minarelli Motori, Fantic Motor
Motorcycles, Ducati Motor, and Padana Sviluppo.
This represents an important acknowledgment of the content quality and development of FMI’s Technical and Training Division,
whose evolution is clearly moving toward expanding FMI’s offerings into highly professional sectors such as this.
These activities also have positive effects on relationships and positioning between the FMI and the motorcycle industry,   115
paving the way for potentially significant synergies in the future.

Other Training Sectors

Motorcycling and Disability
ITS-FMI Project: In collaboration with ITS Foundation, Motor Valley, Emilia-Romagna Region, and Confindustria Emilia-
Romagna, a post-diploma course was launched in 2022 and took its first steps in 2023. This program focuses on mechanical
and mechatronic training in the motorcycle sector. The FMI played a key role in the design phase of the course and its training
program and is now directly and indirectly responsible for most of the course content and specialized technical modules
related to the motorcycle world. The success of this initiative led to the transformation of the program into a permanent school,
along with the doubling of available student placements.

## Pagina 121

SECTION 3 | Sports performance



The Maglia Azzurra              The history of the Maglia Azzurra dates back to the first half of
                                   the 20th century.
                                         In 1911, the Italian Motorcycling Federation was founded, and
                              by 1930, Italian riders Rosolino Grana, Luigi Gilera, and Miro
                                    Maffeis brought home the first national trophy: the Six Days of
                                 Enduro, held that year in Grenoble, France. In its early decades,
                                   the Blue Jersey’s legacy was closely tied to Enduro, thanks to
                                  legendary riders who remain icons of the discipline to this day.

                                         In 1987, the Maglia Azzurra claimed its first international trophy
                                   outside of Enduro, when Diego Bosis, Renato Chiaberto, Carlo
                                  Franco, and Donato Miglio triumphed in the Trial des Nations in
                                Tampere, Finland. In the following years, Italian riders continued
                                     to shine in Trial, standing on the podium alongside powerhouse
                                    nations such as Spain, France, and Great Britain. The success
                                      carried over into Motocross, when in 1999, Andrea Bartolini,
                                    Alessio Chiodi, and Claudio Federici won the Motocross of                                              119
                                  Nations in Brazil—a feat repeated a few years later by Bartolini,
                                     Chiodi, and Alessandro Puzar. The early 2000s marked a period
                                      of continued dominance across multiple disciplines — not only
                                           in Enduro, where Italy has long been a world leader, but also
                                           in other areas such as Supermoto, with six victories in the
                              Supermoto of Nations since 2004.

                                         In 2005, recognizing the growing strength of all national teams, the FMI officially launched the Maglia Azzurra FMI Project,
                                       uniting all Italian national teams under one symbolic jersey — a single identity representing Italy in world events such as the
                                   Trophies of Nations and the Six Days of Enduro. Today, the Golden Roll of Honor includes over 60 titles, encompassing both
                                     senior and youth categories, as well as countless prestigious podiums in every discipline.
                             Beyond titles, Italy’s legacy in off-road sports is also reflected in the international events it organizes, showcasing the enduring
                                 passion and excellence of Italian motorcycling.

## Pagina 122

The Maglia Azzurra 2024

                                     The presentation of the Maglia Azzurra FMI is one of the most anticipated events of the year.
                                     The 2024 national team uniform was unveiled on June 15, 2024, in Maggiora, during the Italian GP of the Motocross World
                                      Championship — first before national and international media, and later before hundreds of fans during the Paddock Show.
                                           At the media presentation, FMI Federal Councillor Armando Sponga, Motocross Committee Coordinator Roberto Rustichelli,
                                   FMI Trainiring Commissioner Thomas Traversini, and 2023 MX2 World Champion Andrea Adamo showcased the new Maglia
                                             Azzurra. In the afternoon, fans witnessed the jersey reveal led by Rustichelli, Traversini, and riders Andrea Adamo, Mattia
                                         Guadagnini, Valerio Lata, and Ferruccio Zanchi — all of whom have proudly worn the Maglia Azzurra in either senior or junior
                                               national teams.




120





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 123

SECTION 3 | Sports performance



The 2024 Junior Motocross World Championship

The Junior Motocross World Championship, held on July 14th in Heerde, Netherlands, was a double-header for the Maglia
Azzurra. The more experienced riders demonstrated their skill, while the younger riders gained experience on a sandy surface
that was very different from their usual terrain. Simone Mancini was among the protagonists in the 125cc class. In race 1,
he finished third, handing over second place to Spaniard Francisco Garcia just a few meters from the finish line. The Italian
talent later redeemed himself by crossing the finish line in the second heat. This success, combined with his top-three finish
in the first heat, earned him second place in the World Championship. Hungary’s Noel Zanocz secured the title. Nicolò Alvisi
achieved a positive eighth-place finish on the day (11th and 9th in the heats), while a feverish Francesco Bellei finished late in
race 1 and was unable to participate in race 2. The 85cc class saw an unfortunate Andrea Uccellini. In race 1, while comfortably
in the top 10, he was forced to retire following a crash. The bruises he sustained prevented him from lining up for race 2.
Edoardo Riganti and David Cracco finished 23rd and 26th, respectively. Levi Townley, son of 2004 MX2 world champion Ben,
was crowned World Champion. It was a useful weekend for the Italians to gain experience in the 65cc class. Antony Montoneri
finished 32nd and Achille Esposito 34th on a day where local rider Kash Van Hamond won the World Championship title in his
category. In the national rankings, Italy placed 11th. The Netherlands won, beating France and Great Britain.
The Italian team was, of all the participants, the one that best promoted environmental sustainability, thus winning the Ride   121
Green Cup.

FIM Enduro Vintage Trophy 2024

                                A well-deserved victory, earned day after day, finally materialized today: at the end of the cross-country heats held in San
                                          Severino Marche, Italy won the FIM Enduro Vintage Trophy for the fourth time. Following the 2019 success of Stefano
                                             Passeri, Mario Rinaldi, and Giò Sala, the team of Giorgio Grasso, Tullio Pellegrinelli, and Enrico Tortoli, with Team Manager
                                      Francesco Mazzoleni, achieved this feat for the third consecutive year, following victories in Portugal in 2022 and Spain in
                                         2023. On Wednesday, September 4th, the race began with the acceleration test, which saw the Maglia Azzurra finish in second
                                              place. On Thursday and Friday, dedicated to pure regularity, the Italian team was unrivalled, decisively defeating their rivals,
                                                              first Germany and then France. In the traditional final Motocross race, our national team riders preserved their bikes and
                                         avoided any possible risks, thus managing to win the prestigious Vintage Veterans Trophy.
                                            Enrico Tortoli and Giorgio Grasso won the A3 and B3 classes, respectively, while Tullio Pellegrinelli finished second in the C3
                                                class. Italy, awarded on the podium by FIM representatives and FMI Vice President Rocco Lopardo, won the Vintage Veterans
                                         Trophy with a 5’10” lead over France and 7’ over the Czech Republic. In the Silver Vase, Moto Club Manzano 3 won, ahead of
                                          Pantera 1 and Sorci Verdi B. In the Vintage Club category, the French team from Team Cardel France won, ahead of Team Lmo
                                                         Aofficial.it and the German team from Club 1 Kramer. The AMCF C women’s team was awarded the Vintage Women’s Club.
                                    The Italian class winners were: Federico Fregnan (A0), Marco Bono (A1), Claudio Giuggioli (A2), Andrea Rastrelli (A3), Massimo
122                                             Parrini (B1), Gianfranco Crivellari (B2), Giorgio Grasso (B3), Angelo Signorelli (C0), Massimiliano Luzi (C1), Tullio Pellegrinelli
                                                  (C3), and Mauro Sant (X2).

The 2024 Trial des Nations

A result that looks to the future while confirming the strength of the present. At the 2024 Trial des Nations, held in Pobladura
de Las Regueras, the Maglia Azzurra (Italian National Team) reached the podium in every competition: second place for both
the men’s and women’s teams, and victory in the Challenge category.
TDN MEN’S TEAM
With the top step of the podium claimed by Spain, the Italian team — Matteo Grattarola (Beta), Luca Petrella (Gas Gas), and
Lorenzo Gandola (Beta) — achieved a hard-fought second place after a long and intense battle with France.
In the first lap, section six posed some difficulties for Italy, but the team closed the opening part of the race strongly with 46
penalty points, ahead of France (53) and behind Spain (21).
During the second lap, after an evenly matched first half, the Maglia Azzurra managed to pull ahead, capitalizing on the time
penalties accumulated by the French team. Italy secured second place with a total of 86 points, behind Spain (42) and ahead
of France (106).
TDN WOMEN’S TEAM
A historic result for the Italian women’s team: Andrea Sofia Rabino (Beta), Alessia Bacchetta (Gas Gas), and Martina Gallieni
(Scorpa) achieved second place — the first-ever podium finish in history for the Italian women.
After a few early challenges, the team ended the first lap in third place with 29 penalties, behind Spain (26) and Great Britain (7).

## Pagina 126

In the second lap, a powerful performance from section 10 through section 15, including a streak of zero penalties, proved
                                             decisive for overtaking Spain by just one point.
                                     The final standings saw Great Britain as the winner with 14 penalty points, followed by Italy with 44, and Spain third with 45.
                               CHALLENGE CATEGORY
                                                   In the mixed teams’ competition, composed of young riders, Martina Brandani (Sherco) and Fabio Mazzola (TRRS) brought
                                  Team Italy back to the top of the podium, repeating the success achieved at TDN 2022 in Monza. The victory was never in
                                           doubt: the talented young duo finished with 51 penalty points, 11 points ahead of Norway (62), the defending champions from
                                            the previous year. Germany completed the podium in third place with 92 points.

                                     2024 Supermoto of Nations

                                           At the Carole Circuit, just outside Paris, the race heats of the 2024 Supermoto of Nations took place. In this edition, the
                                           competition returned to having a shared starting grid, while the final standings remained separate. The Italian “Maglia Azzurra”
                                               national team secured 5th place in the main category, scoring 47 points. Victory went to France with 18 points, followed by
                                              Austria and Germany with 22. The Italy Junior team finished fourth with 35 points, while France Jr dominated overall with just
                                     8 points. Germany Jr took second place (16 pts), and Belgium Jr came third (26 pts).
                                   Race 1: The standout of this heat was Elia Sammartin. After a chaotic start, the Maglia Azzurra rider found himself in 6th
                                              position at the end of the first lap, but thanks to a perfect comeback and impressive consistency in lap times—topped off
                                             with an overtaking move of pure speed on the Frenchman Steve Bonnal—Sammartin managed to close the race in 1st place.
                                           Misfortune struck Luca D’Addato, who fell after contact while running 12th, but still managed to finish 17th at the checkered
                                                      flag. As for the Junior riders, Gabriele Monica placed 27th, while Matteo Andreotti was penalized five positions for failing the
                                     sound check, finishing 28th.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

Race 2: Starting from the 18th grid slot, Luca D’Addato delivered a solid and intelligent race, crossing the finish line in 13th
place. Fabrizio Bartolini, after a poor start, climbed up to 10th, but a fall in the closing laps compromised his performance,
leaving him in 22nd place. Tommaso Pompilio and Gabriele Monica finished 23rd and 25th respectively.
Race 3: In the final race, Elia Sammartin reacted quickly at the start and managed to maintain 4th position until the end.
Fabrizio Bartolini fought hard in the mid-pack, finishing 15th at the checkered flag. Further back were Tommaso Pompilio and
Matteo Andreotti, who crossed the line 22nd and 27th respectively.

## Pagina 128

2024 Quadcross and Sidecarcross of Nations

                                A major achievement for Italy, which on Sunday, September 29, at the Quadcross of Nations, captured an excellent third place.
                                                   In the Czech Republic, on the Loket circuit, Simone Mastronardi, Nicolò Roagna, and Patrick Turrini earned a spot on the
                                    podium after intense battles with rival teams. In the Sidecarcross of Nations, Italy finished ninth.
                                     Quadcross of Nations: The Maglia Azzurra Quad’s Day began unluckily.
                                                   In the first heat, Simone Mastronardi and Nicolò Roagna — the latter slowed by a fall — finished 16th and 28th, respectively.
                                                   In the second heat, Patrick Turrini emerged as one of the key contenders, securing a second-place finish, crucial for bringing
                                                        Italy back into contention. Mastronardi finished 13th. In the third and decisive heat, Turrini once again delivered a stellar
                                         performance, finishing second, while Nicolò Roagna crossed the line in 17th place. With these performances, Italy secured
                                                    third place in the Quadcross of Nations, ranking second among European teams. The United States claimed the trophy with
                                     7 points, followed by the Netherlands (41) and Italy (52). It was a tight final classification, with Estonia (52), Great Britain (54),
                                            Argentina (56), and Latvia (59) all finishing close behind the Italian squad.
                                         Sidecarcross of Nations: The Italian team’s growth at the international level continued in this event, won by the Netherlands.
                                                        Italy finished ninth overall. In Race 1, the crew of Federico and Filippo Fiorini took 18th place, while Lorenzo Baldini and
                                          Stefano Tibaldi finished 22nd. The second heat featured Ivo and Ivan Lasagna, who crossed the line 16th, while Baldini and
126                                            Tibaldi placed 20th. In the final race, Federico and Filippo Fiorini finished 17th, while the Lasagna brothers were forced to retire
                                     due to a mechanical failure. Italy closed the competition in ninth place with 93 points.
                                     The Netherlands took the victory, ahead of Great Britain and France.

Italians chose the most inside gate, started well but did not find the right rhythm and halfway through the race, while in 15th

Open) remained within the top 15 in the initial stages, managing to reach 12th place halfway through the race. After stopping
to change goggles on the tenth lap, he climbed back from 19th to 16th position. Andrea Adamo (MX2) started well but did
not find the right feeling and finished 21st. In the last heat Alberto Forato (MXGP) stood out with a consistent race within the
top 10. Twelfth until halfway through, he climbed up to finish the race in 7th place. Andrea Bonacorsi (MX Open) finished 18th
after sixteen laps that saw him close to the top 15. Italy obtained eighth place in the team standings with 70 points. Australia
came first with 26 points, ahead of the United States (29) and the Netherlands (36). On an individual level, Forato finished 6th
in MXGP while Bonacorsi and Adamo placed ninth respectively in the Open and MX2 categories.

## Pagina 130

Six Days of Enduro 2024

                                       Always giving it their all and throwing everything into the mix. The 2024 Six Days of Enduro in Silleda, Spain, saw Moto Club
                                                           Italia demonstrate their strength and win the Club Team Award. The team of Lorenzo Macoritto, Enrico Rinaldi, and Pietro
                                          Scardina was incredibly fast in the Special Stages from start to finish, then closed the deal in the motocross heat. In the
                                   Women’s Trophy, the girls secured fifth place overall. The Trophy and Junior Trophy riders raced on the final day of competition,
                                          aiming to showcase their speed. At the end of the awards ceremony, FMI President Giovanni Copioli received the Six Days
                                         plaque from FIM President Jorge Viegas to symbolize the handover of the organization of the oldest off-road motorcycle
                                            competition, which will be held in Bergamo next year.

## Pagina 132

2024 European Speedway Championship

                                     The Moto Club Lonigo hosted on Sunday, October 20, the prestigious end-of-season event, the European Speedway Pairs
                                      Championship. Victory went to Denmark, ahead of the favoured Poland and Great Britain. Italy finished in fifth place, behind
                                               Finland. Seven nations took part in the event: Italy, as the host country, was automatically entered along with the top three
                                            nations from 2023 (Poland, Denmark, Latvia) and the top two finishers from the mid-August semifinal (Germany and Great
                                                      Britain). The national team’s training commissioner, Alessandro Dalla Valle, relied on the experience of Paco Castagna and
                                            Nicolas Vicentin as the designated starting pair, and on the young Matteo Boncinelli as the reserve rider ready to step in for
                                                 either of the two Azzurri.
                                        Adverse weather conditions, with heavy rain in the days leading up to the race, caused a three-hour delay to the scheduled
                                                    start to allow the track machinery to make the surface as uniform as possible. Italy began its competition against Great Britain.
                                        Leader Tom Brennan surprised the Italian duo at the start and maintained first place until the checkered flag. In the second
                                              heat, Castagna and Vicentin faced the pre-race favourites, the Polish tandem of Mateusz Cierniak and Bartlomiej Kowalski.
                                             Cierniak took the lead, Castagna followed closely through the first two turns but then lost ground to the two-time Under-21
                                          world champion. Vicentin was unable to avoid finishing fourth, behind Kowalski. Against Latvia, missing its star rider Andzejs
                                        Lebedevs, the reigning European individual champion, Italy had an excellent opportunity to recover points in the standings
130                                 and move closer to the podium zone. The Baltic rider Kostigovs produced a flawless heat, leading from start to finish, with
                                       Castagna and Vicentin finishing second and third. In the sixteenth heat, Italy abandoned its hopes of a podium finish following
                                         Castagna’s disqualification for a second false start, which resulted in his exclusion from the heat. The German duo, Blodorn
                                     and Wassermann, prevailed over Vicentin, who finished third. Castagna redeemed himself against Finland, giving Italy its only
                                        win of the day, ahead of Mustonen and Vuolas, with Vicentin in fourth place.

                                                        FMI TRAINING COMMISSIONER                                ALESSANDRO DALLA VALLE

                                     2024 European Enduro Championship

                                     The European Enduro Championship concluded in Woltersdorf, Germany, with the final double round, which, as per tradition,
                                             also served as the team event. Team Italy – guided by FMI Training Commissioner Cristian Rossi and FMI Trainer Alex Zanni –
                                                lined up with Riccardo Fabris, Davide Mei, Riccardo Pasquato, and Davide Soreca. Both on Saturday and Sunday, the riders





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 133

SECTION 3 | Sports performance



faced a Cross Test through the woods with continuous up-and-down sections; an Extreme Test featuring technical passages
over rocks and logs; and an Enduro Test, also through wooded terrain with steep slopes. The entire loop was repeated three
times. The Italian team immediately stood out, proving to be the best formation on the first day of competition with a total time
of 2h43’13”. France, in second place, trailed by 2’15”, while Germany, in third, was 2’21” behind. On Day 2, held on Sunday,
the fastest were the French with a time of 2h44’50”, ahead of Germany (+31”) and Italy (+50”) — a gap that did not affect the
overall standings. Team Italy ultimately won the European title with a combined time of 5h28’54”, ahead of France (+1’25”) and
Germany (+2’01”).
Notably, on Day 2, Riccardo Fabris was forced to withdraw after suffering a concussion and a fracture to his left hand following
a crash. Several Italian riders also shone individually, closing the season as European Champions:
•   Pata Talento Azzurro FMI rider Riccardo Pasquato won the European Youth title.
•   Davide Soreca clinched the continental championship in the E1 class.
•   Maurizio Micheluz triumphed in the Senior category, securing his fourteenth European title.
Also worth mentioning is the victory of the Moto Club Gaerne, crowned champion in the “Trade Team” class.

2024 Mediterranean Trophy

The 25th edition of the Mediterranean Trophy took place on Saturday, November 9th and Sunday, November 10th in Pernes-
les-Fontaines, France, where the Maglia Azzurra took third place on the podium behind second-place France and winners
Spain. The Mediterranean Trophy, first held in 1993, is a national motocross team competition. Only riders who have not scored
points in the FIM World Championship and/or European Championship and Junior Cup in the current year are eligible to enter.
FMI Training Commissioner Thomas Traversini called up Mirko Dal Bosco and Mario Tamai for MX1 and Vincenzo Bove and
Andrea Roberti for MX2. After Saturday’s ballot, where Italy was drawn in fourth place, the Maglia Azzurra faced Sunday’s two
races with determination. During the morning warm-up, Mirko dal Bosco suffered a crash resulting in a dislocated left shoulder,
significantly limiting his performance.
Race 1: Dal Bosco and Roberti, due to initial contact with other riders, were forced to restart from the back of the field. Bove,
after a promising start, also found himself at the back of the field following a crash that forced him to grit his teeth. Tamai, who
had a slow start, staged an excellent comeback to fifth place. Dal Bosco finished eighth with a truly remarkable second half

## Pagina 134

of the race after his warm-up crash. Roberti remained undaunted and crossed the checkered flag in 11th place, followed by
                                    Bove in 12th.
                                    Race 2: Another unfortunate heat for the Maglia Azzurra, with Dal Bosco suffering a crash that forced him to retire due to a
                                           recurrence of his shoulder problem, while the other three Italian riders rode a comeback race; Tamai established a strong pace
                                        from mid-race onwards, finishing in eighth place. Roberti finished ninth, Bove 11th. Italy finished in third place with 28 points,
                                        behind France (15) and Spain (10). In the category’s standings, Mario Tamai and Mirko Dal Bosco secured an excellent second
                                          place in MX1, while in MX2, Andrea Roberti and Vincenzo Bove finished on the third step of the podium.
