## Pagina 138

The Commission for the              In 2024, the Commission for the Development of Sports Activities (CSAS) carried out its work starting from the early months
       Development of Sports              of the year, operating both in the off-road disciplines and in Circuit Racing, continuing the promotion projects included in its
          Activities                           mission.

                                          During 2024, every effort was made to pursue all the initiatives outlined in the CSAS Regulation, which are summarized below:

                                                           •   The Hobby Sport Project, divided into Hobby Sport Young Courses, aimed at children – including those without an FMI
                                        Membership Card – under the age of 15, and Hobby Sport Master Events, which require membership and a medical
                                                        certificate for non-competitive sports activities. The courses carried out were:
                                             –  96 Hobby Sport Master Events (compared to 78 in 2023);
                                             –  364 Hobby Sport Young Courses (compared to 78 in 2023)

                                                           •     It is worth noting that among the 408 Hobby Sport Young Courses in 2023 were also included the weekly courses organized
                                         by the CSAS in Lombardy, Tuscany, and Marche (for the off-road sector) and in Emilia Romagna (for the Circuit Racing
                                                      sector). These weekly sessions were an integral part of the “Hobby Sport / First Steps Off-Road and Circuit Racing”
136                                               Project, which involved 120 children who received the MiniSport Card through regular weekly activities held during March,
                                                           April, May, June, September, and October. Considering that this project was limited to the 2023 sports season, and that
                                                the number of 2024 courses, although lower, is consistent with those not linked to the project, the educational activity
                                                   carried out in 2024 is fully aligned with the Commission’s objectives set at the beginning of the year.
                                                           •   For 2024, the Hobby Card was confirmed, with the same issue procedures as in 2023: It is issued only to children under
                                        15 years old, who are not FMI members, and enrolled in a Hobby Sport Young Course through the online procedure. The
                                      Hobby Card is issued free of charge and can be used during the same sports season to apply for an FMI Membership Card
                                           (Member, Sport, or MiniSport) with a €10.00 credit applied to its cost.
                                                           •   Expansion of the regional network of Representatives, many of whom hold the federal qualification of Off-Road Riding
                                                    Instructor or Sports Trainer. The Commission, in collaboration with the FMI Training Sector, organizes training and refresher
                                            courses for its Representatives to enhance, among other things, their interaction with children and parents.
                                                           •   The collaboration with the FMI Training Sector was confirmed, developing a growth and initiation project for off-road
                                                        riding, combining Hobby Sport activities with New Entry Courses and Theoretical-Practical Courses (CTP). This structured
                                          approach allows for a training path starting from Hobby Sport, continuing through New Entry courses, and culminating in
                                                the CTPs.
                                                           •   Collaboration with the FMI Training Sector also extended to the coordination of regional youth activities in several areas
                                                    of Italy.
                                                           •   Collaboration with the Circuit Racing Committee, particularly in the Minimoto sector, where the CSAS developed a program

    to introduce children (from age 6) to the discipline. With the support of qualified Sports Trainers in circuit racing, the

    Trophy and Mountaintrial Trophy were introduced in 2024. These were open to all FMI members participating in such

•    In 2024, the Commission organized a training course for FMI members to obtain the qualification of IGAST – Informer
    for Amateur Riding for the Protection of the Territory. This role was created by the Federation to allow FMI-affiliated
   Moto Clubs to organize off-road excursions led by qualified guides, trained both in technical aspects and in appropriate
    behaviour during off-road riding.

## Pagina 143

The sustainability of major FMI sporting events

## Pagina 144

In recent years, the Italian Motorcycling Federation has developed significant expertise in developing Strategic Sustainability Plans for national and international
         sporting events.
         This section describes the processes and activities related to two major events the Federation organized in 2024, with a particular commitment to sustainability.

       The 77th FIM Rally            On the occasion of the 77th FIM Rally, held in Chianciano Terme from June 26 to 28, 2024, the Italian Motorcycling Federation
                                              (FMI) developed and published a Strategic Sustainability Plan, with the goal of organizing an event that is sustainable and
                                               respectful of the environmental, economic, and social dimensions.

                                     The sustainability framework of the 2024 FIM Rally was inspired by the 17 Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) of the UN
                                     2030 Agenda, with significant reference to the European standard UNI ISO 20121 (2013), which defines the sustainability
                                          standard for event management systems, and to the Guideline UNI ISO 26000, published in October 2020.
                                    As a first step, ten core values were identified to represent the foundational principles of the event and to serve as guidance
                                                   for behaviour throughout the rally. These values constitute the Charter of Values of the FIM Rally:

                                                           •    Internationality
142                                                     •   Respect
                                                           •   Sustainability
                                                           •   Solidarity
                                                           •   Passion
                                                           •   Loyalty
                                                           •   Responsibility
                                                           •   Sportsmanship
                                                           •   Safety
                                                           •   Inclusion

                                           Next, attention was focused on analysing the context from social, economic, and environmental perspectives. This analysis
                                        enabled a detailed mapping of stakeholders, including:

                                                           •   Local Health Authority (ASL) of Chianciano Terme
                                                           •   Environmental associations
                                                           •   Cultural associations
                                                           •   Fitness centres
                                                           •   Local community: residents of Chianciano Terme




        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

•   Croce Verde (Green Cross)
•  CUFAA (Carabinieri Forest and Environmental Unit Command)
•   Local authorities: Municipality of Chianciano Terme, Province of Siena, Region of Tuscany
•   National institutions
•   FMI (Italian Motorcycling Federation)
•   Parents of student volunteers
•   ISPRA (Italian Institute for Environmental Protection and Research)
•   Media

## Pagina 148

Certification of the Italian         The Italian Speed Championship (CIV) is the most important national motorcycle road racing championship, organized by
         Circuit Racing Championship       the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) since 2000. The event takes place annually at the most prestigious circuits in Italy,
         (CIV)                           namely the Imola (BO), Misano (RN), Mugello (FI), and Vallelunga (Campagnano di Roma, RM) racetracks. The championship
                                             consists of 12 rounds held over 6 racing weekends, taking place between April and October. The main competitors participate
                                                     in the Moto3, Superbike, Supersport 600, Premoto3, and Supersport 300 classes. The level of competition is remarkably high,
                                        as evidenced by the numerous motorcycle manufacturers and the wide range of stakeholders involved in the championship.

                                    From a sustainability perspective, the Speed Committee of the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) is committed to organizing
                                            the Italian Speed Championship (CIV) while ensuring respect for the environment, territories, and local communities. To this
                                          end, a Sustainability Policy was published in 2024 and implemented through the definition of the “Strategic Plan for the
                                      Development of the CIV Sustainability System”, establishing the following objectives:

                                                           •   Adhere to the principles of sustainable development and social responsibility of organizations (ISO 26000:2010), and
                                                 contribute to achieving the United Nations (UN) 2030 Agenda Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs);
                                                           •   Ensure compliance with current regulations on the environment, noise, labour, health, and safety;
                                                           •   Promote the principles and values established in the FMI Charter of Values;
146                                                     •   Engage stakeholders on relevant sustainability aspects of the CIV;
                                                           •   Promote health and safety and prevent the use of prohibited substances;
                                                           •   Reduce the environmental impact of the CIV in accordance with the provisions of the FMI Environmental Regulations;
                                                           •   Minimize consumption and waste production, favouring the use of green energy;
                                                           •   Optimize the management of natural resources;
                                                           •   Encourage more sustainable behaviours among suppliers;
                                                           •   Enhance the economic benefits generated by the event;
                                                           •   Ensure access to and enjoyment of the event for all individuals, without discrimination, with particular attention to vulnerable
                                              groups;
                                                           •   Serve as a best practice and promote sustainability in national and international sporting events.

                                        To achieve these objectives, several operational tools have been developed, including:

                                               Team                                                       Spectator
                                               Handbook                                          Handbook





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

Financial statements
                                    The Financial Statements are an informational document aimed at determining and presenting the Operating
                                             Result, calculated as the algebraic sum of costs and revenues.
                                         For 2024, the Operating Result of the Federation recorded a profit of €1,547,010.53, once again showing
                                     growth compared to 2023.

                                   Compared to the previous year, the FMI further increased its revenues, with the Total Production Value rising from €25,267,786.41
                                              to €26,091,768.76.
                                        Focusing on Production Costs, these followed a similar trend to 2023, increasing from €24,367,789.81 to €24,834,339.52.

## Pagina 158

The added value                The Added Value represents the wealth generated by the Federation, identified as the difference between the core production
                                     and all intermediate production costs attributable to the activities carried out by the central and regional structures of the
                                             Federation.

                                                After deducting the results from non-core activities and the total amount of depreciation and provisions, the Net Global Added
                                           Value (VAGN) is obtained. This value represents the increase in wealth created by the Federation through its service-related
                                                      activities, which is then distributed among all its internal and external stakeholders.

                                     The determination of the Added Value is particularly significant, as  it provides the accounting link between the Financial
                                        Statements and the Sustainability Report.

                                          For its calculation, several items from the statutory financial statements were reclassified to accurately determine the wealth
                                         generated within the FMI system. Subsequently, the added value was allocated among the various stakeholder groups of the
                                           Federation to illustrate its distribution.

                                    The distribution of value added among FMI stakeholders
156
                                     The Net Global Added Value (VAGN) of the FMI  for the year 2024 amounts to €14,546,346, marking an increase of 8.44%
                                    compared to 2023.
                                     The VAGN is distributed among the Federation’s various stakeholders as follows:
                                          Personnel – € 7.071.956,80
                                           Federation – € 1.547.010,53
                                             External Donations and Contributions – € 5.550.171,96
                                             Financiers – 183.883,06
                                             Public Administration (Taxes and Duties) – € 193.323,00





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 160

For the fiscal year 2024, the Net Global Added Value (VAGN) of the FMI was distributed as follows:
                                         •  € 7.071.956,80 to Personnel (employees and non-employees), this category received the largest share of the generated
                                                   value. It includes all individuals who collaborate with the Federation on a paid basis.
                                                       Specifically, personnel are divided into:
                                             –  Employees, with fixed-term or permanent contracts;
                                             –  Non-employee personnel, such as consultants, occasional collaborators, and members of central and regional
                                                      federation commissions.
                                        The remuneration of personnel can be further broken down into two components:
                                             –  The first includes all items representing the direct economic benefits arising from the employment relationship with the
                                                   Federation (e.g., ordinary and additional wages).
                                             –  The second refers to social contributions paid by the Federation on behalf of its workers.
                                         •  € 5.550.171,96 in External Donations and Contributions, representing the value distributed to sports associations and
                                               clubs to support the organization of sporting activities.
                                         •  € 193.323,0000 to the Public Administration, through the payment of direct and indirect taxes. This represents the economic
                                                   benefit that the public sector receives from the Federation’s activities as a contribution to national public spending.
158                                     •  € 183.883,06 to Financiers, represented by the Italian credit system and CONI, which provide capital and financial support
                                                  to the Federation.
                                         •   €1.547,010.53 to the Federation itself, considered as an independent stakeholder. This amount corresponds to the net
                                                operating surplus for the year 2024.

Environmental performance

## Pagina 170

In 2024, the FMI Environment Commission continued its regular work to analyse and understand all aspects of motorcycling and their impact on the natural
        environment. It also developed novel solutions to directly reduce this impact and work on its prevention.


        Environmental Commission        The FMI’s Environment and Off-Road Regulations Commission promotes sustainable motorcycling by monitoring and reducing
         report                              the environmental impacts of off-road and other activities through mitigation and compensation actions.

                                          Environmental Commission’s principal areas of intervention include:
                                         •   Training and awareness-raising for practitioners and event organizers
                                         •   Activities of FMI Environmental Delegates
                                         •   Environmental monitoring and environmental compensation projects
                                         •  Development and updating of environmental regulations and guidelines

                                      Performed activities in 2024

                                                 1.  Training of Environmental Delegates
168
                                                   In 2024, the Commission launched a training program for new Environmental Delegates, with the aim of expanding the
                                           environmental monitoring network during events.

                                     The training will be conducted in two phases:
                                         •  Remote training: launched in December 2024.
                                         •   Practical meetings: two meetings scheduled for 2025: January 25, 2025, in Milan for the northern area and January 26,
                                             2025, in Rome for the central-southern area.
                                      These meetings will involve 40 candidate Stewards of the Sport.

                                                 2.  2024 FMI’s Environmental Delegates’ activities

                                     41 Environmental Delegates active in 2024.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

motorcycle events. The assignments were divided as follows:

to data collection and verifying compliance with FMI environmental regulations.

## Pagina 172

To assess the sustainability of the monitored events, the Environmental Delegates compile a checklist based on 33 environmental
                                          parameters.
                                   Key Performance Indicators

                                Among the main indicators monitored, we include:
                                         •   Public services: signage, accessibility, restroom facilities, and structures for people with disabilities.
                                         •   General environmental management: proper use of motorcycle washing areas, availability of environmental mats, waste
                                        and wastewater management.
                                         •   Refuelling area: safety standards, spill containment structures, soil conditions.
                                         •   Environmental communication: information provided to the public and participants, digitalization of communication tools.
                                         •  Food service facilities: waste management, hygiene, and sustainability practices.
                                         •  Noise and acoustic impact: positioning of loudspeakers and mitigation of environmental disturbance.
                                            Calculation of the Environmental Performance Index (EPI)

170                                 Each indicator is assessed according to the following scale:

                                                 ASSESSMENT                SCORE

                                              GOOD                        +2

                                          ACCEPTABLE BUT CAN BE IMPROVED           +1

                                               NOT SUITABLE                       –1

                                              NOT APPLICABLE             0 (excluded from the
                                                                                                                    calculation)

                                         •   Total Environmental Score: sum of the points assigned across all applicable indicators (maximum 33 × 2 = 66 theoretical
                                                      points).
                                         •  Weighted Average Score: average of the scores on applicable parameters only, excluding those marked as “not applicable.”

                                              Calculation example:
                                                           •   Applicable parameters: 28




        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 173

SECTION 5 | Environmental performance



•   Ratings: 18 “good” (2 points), 8 “acceptable” (1 point), 2 “unsuitable” (–1 point)
•   Total points: 36 + 8 – 2 = 42
•   Average weighted score: 42 / 28 = 1.50
This system guarantees an objective and comparable assessment of the environmental sustainability of different events.

The boxplot graph shows the distribution of values for each category (Specialty: Enduro, Motocross, Trials). The graph displays
                                            the median, variability, and individual values, facilitating comparisons between the three Off-Road specialties.
                                            Several parametric and nonparametric statistical tests were then performed to compare the three specialties.

                                       Here are the results:

                               ANOVA F-test:
                                                           •   P-value = 0.047 (< 0.05)   Significant
                                                           •   Conclusion: There is a statistically significant difference between the means of the three specialties

                                            Kruskal-Wallis Test (non-parametric alternative):
                                                           •   P-value = 0.049 (< 0.05)   Significant
                                                           •   Confirm the ANOVA result

                                          Normality Test (Shapiro-Wilk):

SECTION 5 | Environmental performance



•   Enduro: P-value = 0.132 → Normal distribution
•   Motocross: P-value = 0.054 → Normal distribution (borderline)
•    Trial: P-value = 0.750 → Normal distribution

Therefore, the three specialties—Enduro, Motocross, and Trial—have statistically different means (p < 0.05). The data follow a
normal distribution, so ANOVA is appropriate. Trial has the highest mean, followed by Enduro and then Motocross.

ANOVA Test
              TEST                      F-VALUE                P-VALUE                       SIGNIFICANT_α=0.05

         ANOVA F-TEST                      3.9058                     0.047                          TRUE

                                                                                  173
        SHAPIRO-WILK TRIAL                    0.983                       0.75                            FALSE

Trial events could present higher environmental quality indicators than Motocross and Enduro for assorted reasons linked to
the very nature of the sport and the impact on the environment:

Lower impact on the terrain
•    In Trial, riders navigate natural or artificial obstacles without racing at high speed over long distances.
•   Tracks are short and highly technical, with slow and controlled movements, causing less soil erosion and compaction
   compared to motocross or enduro, where bikes cover long distances at high speed on rough terrain.

More confined and controlled courses
•    Trial competitions often take place in clearly defined and controlled areas, reducing use and impact on large natural
    surfaces.
•   This facilitates environmental management: lower risk of disturbing sensitive habitats or causing widespread damage.

Lower noise and dust emissions
•    Trial bikes are designed to have moderate power and operate at low RPM, generating less noise and dust than more

## Pagina 176

powerful motocross or enduro bikes.

                                    Ease of monitoring and management
                                                           •  Due to the technical nature and limited course length, environmental delegates can more easily monitor and implement
                                                   mitigation measures (waste collection, protection of sensitive areas, signage) effectively.

                                          Greater regulatory attention and specific guidelines
                                                           •    Trial often follows stricter environmental guidelines requiring careful attention to the territory, facilitating compliance with
                                              environmental standards.

                                                In summary:
                                                   Trials = lower impact, easier management, and less degraded environments
                                      Motocross/Enduro = more widespread impact, higher speeds, and terrain wear

                                      These factors contribute to environmental quality indicators for trial races often being more positive than for other, more
                                               “intensive” off-road sports.
174
                                                 3.  Environmental monitoring and environmental compensation projects

                                                   In 2024, the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI), through its Commission for Environment and Off-Road Regulations, further
                                          strengthened its environmental sustainability initiatives. Among these, a significant role was played by environmental offset
                                                projects, aimed at reducing or neutralizing the CO₂ emissions generated by off-road motorcycling events and other federation
                                                      activities.

                                          Objectives of Compensation Activities
                                                           •   Offset the greenhouse gas emissions generated by official FMI events and other activities (especially off-road and
                                             motorcycle tourism).
                                                           •   Promote a culture of environmental responsibility among organizers, riders, and the public.
                                                           •   Ensure compliance with the principles of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs – Agenda 2030), with particular
                                                reference to Goal 13 – Climate Action.

                                    FIM Rally Project – Chianciano Terme 2024
                                      Date and venue: 26–28 June, Chianciano Terme, Siena
                                            Tree Planting Initiative – (21 March 2024)





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 177

SECTION 5 | Environmental performance



•   Organized jointly by the FMI Tourism and Leisure Commission and the FMI Environmental and Off-Road Regulations
   Commission, the activity involved 80 local primary school children.
•   Over 30 white mulberry trees were planted, one for each of the participating nations (approx. 30), with the goal of offsetting
    the CO₂ emissions of the Rally.

The initiative aimed to symbolically offset the CO₂ emissions generated by the event (transport, logistics, and motorcycles
involved). Furthermore, the project engaged the local community, raising awareness among young generations on environmental
issues.

“Misano Green CIV 2024” Project
The Misano World Circuit, in collaboration with the FMI Environmental and Off-Road Regulations Commission, launched a
sustainability plan (2024–2026) based on the annual planting of 40 trees, aimed at compensating the CO₂ emissions generated
by circuit activities and events linked to the Italian Circuit Racing Championship (CIV).
On 7 March 2024, 40 trees were planted near the Parco della Greppa. Around 70 primary school children took part in educational
sessions on sustainability.
FIM Enduro Vintage Trophy 2024 Project                                                         175
From the 4th to 7th September 2024, Camerino (MC) hosted the FIM Enduro Vintage Trophy 2024, an international event
dedicated to classic Enduro motorcycles

Environmental Monitoring conducted:
•  Noise impact: sound level measurements of historic motorcycles confirmed values within sustainability limits.
•   Air quality: environmental parameters recorded, ensuring no significant territorial impacts.
•   Biodiversity: analysis of flora and fauna in the areas concerned showed reversible or sustainable impacts.
•   Technical documentation: a complete environmental report published on the FMI website on 21 November 2024.
The investigations highlighted the environmental sustainability of the event, particularly in terms of noise impact, already
confirmed in past editions. Impacts on biodiversity (both flora and fauna) proved fully sustainable or entirely reversible.

No critical issues emerged during the monitoring phase: the event operated in compliance with environmental standards
and with full respect for the territory. Thanks to careful event planning and controlled operational conditions, the FIM Enduro
Vintage Trophy 2024 met the required sustainability standards, with manageable and tolerable acoustic and environmental
impacts.

## Pagina 178

4.  Update of Environmental Regulations

                                     The Commission has updated the FMI Environmental Regulations, introducing new guidelines for the environmental
                                   management of motorcycle events.
                                                   In 2024, the FMI Environmental and Off-Road Regulations Commission reinforced its commitment to responsible motorcycling,
                                      by starting the training of new FMI Environmental Delegates, updating regulations, monitoring events directly in the field, and
                                         promoting a progressively more sustainable environmental impact.

                                                 5.  Seminars - conferences

                                    As part of the environmental awareness activities coordinated by the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI), two specific events
                                      were held in 2024:
                                                           •   Mini-conference during the FMI Minimoto Championship round at the Happy Valley Circuit in Cervia (RA), on April 19, 2024.
                                                           •   Event on the sustainability of motorcycle tourism in the Bergamo valleys, held in Selvino (BG) on June 8, 2024, titled “Two
                                         Wheels to Drive Economy and Sustainability”.
176                                            6.  FMI and Eni: a Strategic Agreement for Sustainability in Motorcycling
                                                   In November 2023, the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) and Eni signed a highly significant collaboration agreement, aimed
                                                at studying and implementing solutions, products, and technologies focused on sustainability in the motorcycling sector.
                                                After a preliminary analysis phase, six areas of intervention were identified, with the goal of developing eco-friendly products,
                                              solutions for the remediation of contaminated land, as well as initiatives to improve energy efficiency and the use of renewable
                                         energy in Moto Club buildings, racetracks, and other facilities belonging to FMI affiliates. The agreement, coordinated by Eni
                                           Sustainable B2B, involved in particular Eni Plenitude, Versalis, and Eni Rewind.
                                     The collaboration also included sustainable mobility solutions, developed by Enilive, through joint initiatives and training
                                          sessions for FMI trainers and officials.
                                       Through this agreement, the Italian Motorcycling Federation has pursued its commitment to the sustainability of its activities
                                             with renewed conviction and determination, making Eni a key strategic partner in this journey.

                                                 7.  FMI Environment Award

                                    As established by the FMI Environmental Regulations, every year the Environmental Commission assigns the Environment
                                    Award to the Moto Club or organization that has distinguished itself, during the season, for its attention to environmental issues
                                                     in the management of a federal motorcycle event.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 179

SECTION 5 | Environmental performance



2021 Environment Award

The 2021 Environment Award was presented during the Supermoto National round in Ortona on July 3, 2022, and was
assigned to MC Ufo Frascati for the organization of the Viterbo round of the 2021 Supermoto Internationals.
Before one of the race heats, on the starting grid, the Coordinator of the Environmental Commission Giancarlo Strani, together
with FMI Vice President Rocco Lopardo and the Coordinator of the Supermoto Committee Attilio Pignotti, delivered the
recognition to the President of MC Ufo Frascati, Maurizio Lucarini.

2022 Environment Award

The 2022 Environment Award was officially announced on February 16, 2023, during the press conference launching the 2023
season at the Misano World Circuit.
After reviewing the environmental checklists prepared by the Environmental Delegates, the Commission decided to assign
the recognition to the Misano World Circuit Marco Simoncelli for its commitment and focus on environmental sustainability,
demonstrated both in organizing the Women’s European Cup and in supporting FMI in various sustainability initiatives. Among
these, the standout project was the compensation of the entire CO₂ emissions of the 2021 CIV Championship, named CIV
Green.                                                                              177
The Misano World Circuit received the award after a careful selection by the Environmental Commission, which also involved
four other deserving organizations: MC Ceva and MC Ponte San Giovanni (organizers of two Enduro Championship rounds),
MC Bergamo (Enduro Major), and MC A. Fagioli (Italian Motocross Pro).

2023 Environment Award

The Moto Club Bergamo won the 2023 Environment Award, assigned by the Environmental Commission for the excellent
organization—especially in terms of sustainability—of the Italian Trial Championship held on June 25, 2023, in Piazzatorre (BG).
The FMI selected the Bergamo Moto Club event from a shortlist of three competitions: in addition to the Italian Trial, the Centro
Italia Trial Trophy in Montevarchi on October 1 (organized by MC Brilli Peri) and the Italian Motocross Prestige in Monterosato
on May 14 (organized by MC Ippogrifo).
The Bergamo club received the award following a thorough evaluation by the Environmental Commission of the environmental
checklists prepared by the Environmental Delegates. A common positive aspect across all three events was the strong focus
on environmental issues and the sustainability of the competitions.

## Pagina 180

FMI Environment Award 2024

                                A prestigious recognition, highlighting the commitment to environmental protection and the promotion of sustainable
                                            motorcycling. The Moto Club Bergamo won the Environment Award, assigned by the FMI Environmental Commission for
                                            the excellent organization—particularly in terms of sustainability—of the Italian Trial Championship held on June 25, 2024,
                                                     in Piazzatorre (BG). The FMI selected the Bergamo Moto Club event from a shortlist of three competitions: in addition to the
                                                      Italian Trial, the Centro Italia Trial Trophy in Montevarchi on October 1 (organized by MC Brilli Peri) and the Italian Motocross
                                             Prestige in Monterosato on May 14 (organized by MC Ippogrifo). The Bergamo club received the award following a thorough
                                             evaluation by the Environmental Commission of the environmental checklists prepared by the Environmental Delegates. A
                                                positive element, common to all three events, was the strong focus on environmental issues and the sustainability of the
                                            competitions.

                                   FMI at the Forefront in Umbria

                                                   In light of recent legislative initiatives aimed at liberalizing certain trail routes related to hunting and two- and four-wheel
                                            sports activities, with and without engines, the Italian Motorcycling Federation (FMI) took action with the Region of Umbria
178                                          to present its expertise on the proper management and sustainable use of the environment, in order to protect its Members
                                     and sporting events.
                                     The FMI requested and obtained a meeting with the Regional Presidency to open a constructive and collaborative dialogue
                                    on its research activities and to illustrate ongoing collaborations dedicated to environmental protection, carried out with
                                               Universities, the Carabinieri Forest Corps (CUFA), and the Italian Institute for Environmental Protection and Research
                                           (ISPRA).
                                     The FMI Environmental Commission has been working for years to collect data concerning the impact of its activities,
                                             with significant research carried out specifically in the Umbria region. At the same time, the FMI is engaged in training and
                                          awareness-raising within its structures on issues related to the sustainability of motorcycling.
                                        Being listed in the National Register of Civil Protection Associations, FMI also provides its territorial organizational structures
                                     and its motorcycling volunteers for environmental protection. These are deployed not only in cases of natural disasters, but
                                             also for territorial safeguarding, forest fire prevention, search, and rescue operations, and to reach inaccessible areas—as has
                                             already been effectively carried out in other Italian regions, often in cooperation with the Carabinieri Forest Corps.
                                         With the intention of representing the interests of the many Moto Clubs and FMI Members in the Umbria region, a meeting
                                  was requested to contribute to the ongoing debate on the legislative initiative, which clarifies existing rules by limiting the ban
                                    on motor vehicles on trails and mule tracks only to areas where prohibition signs are displayed. We will keep you informed of
                                         developments, which we hope will be positive.





        FMI | Sustainability Report 2024

## Pagina 181

SECTION 5 | Environmental performance



Historic Motorcycles and the Environment. The FMI Study Protocol

The FMI Environmental Commission – Services and Development sector, in coordination with the FMI Historic Register,
developed a study protocol aimed at collecting bibliographic data on motorcycle emissions (both acoustic and atmospheric),
along with measurements taken during motorcycle gatherings or races throughout 2023. This work was carried out in
collaboration with specialized institutions and universities.
The FMI Report 01/2024 summarizes the findings of a year of studies and field investigations, conducted thanks to the
contribution of federal environmental stewards, the technical steward’ group, sound measurement commissioners, ISPRA,
and the University of Perugia.
The document provides a detailed overview of the environmental parameters of “vintage” races, gatherings of historic
motorcycles, and the environmental impact of these vehicles. The overall picture shows a negligible impact of such historic
events, with no significant differences compared to modern motorcycles.
This represents an additional knowledge asset produced by FMI, aimed at sharing with enthusiasts valuable insights on current
environmental issues.

## Pagina 183

FMI’s sustainable improvemen

### Testo nativo breve conservato

FMI’s sustainable improvement

## Pagina 184

In 2017, the Federation developed a five-year strategic sustainability plan, structured around three core priorities, each supported by specific areas of intervention.
         This plan reached further consolidation in 2022, delivering noteworthy results in the social, economic, sporting, and environmental fields for the FMI.
        At the conclusion of the 2017–2022 strategic cycle, the Federation launched a reflection process to define a new sustainability strategy, with the aim of ensuring
         continuity in the achievements obtained. A key element of this process will be the voluntary adherence to the principles and guidelines set forth by the new Directive
         (EU) 2022/2464.
       The Directive (EU) 2022/2464, better known as the Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD), represents a major step toward standardization and
        transparency in sustainability reporting across the European Union. Introduced to expand and replace the previous Non-Financial Reporting Directive (NFRD),
         the CSRD requires companies exceeding certain thresholds to provide reliable and comparable information on their environmental, social, and governance (ESG)
        impact.
        With a view to advancing its sustainable development, including its capacity to measure performance, the FMI will compare its existing performance indicators with
        those set by the CSRD, in order to assess the feasibility of providing stakeholders with more precise and comprehensive sustainability information.

       The Double Materiality Approach
      Among the key innovations introduced by the CSRD is the adoption of the principle of double materiality, along with the requirement to have reported data verified
       by independent third parties, thereby strengthening the credibility and reliability of the disclosed information.
        Double materiality is an approach that calls on organizations to assess not only the impact of their activities on the environment and society, but also the influence
182    of external factors on their financial performance. This concept, adopted under the European Green Deal, promotes a two-way perspective: on the one hand, it
        considers how business operations affect the environment (“inside-out”), and on the other, how environmental and social changes may impact the company itself
          (“outside-in”).
          In light of this, the FMI will initiate a process of review and renewed engagement with its stakeholders, formally adopting the principle of double materiality.

       Double Materiality Topics 2025
       Governance

        Ethics and Integrity
            •   Anti-corruption measures
            •  Code of Ethics
            •   Whistleblowing system
            •   Organizational and Management Model (MOG under Legislative Decree 231/2001)
         Internal Governance
            •   Democratic representation of members
            •   Rotation and composition of governing bodies
            •   Gender inclusion within the federation’s structure
